| **manga-ocr** (MEASURED-LOCAL, CPU) | 0.562 | 0.481 | 0.000 | 0.968 | 10.6 | 1.06 | 952 | no | Apache-2.0 | yes |
| **Surya 2** (LIT/UNMEASURED-local) | — | — | — | — | n/a (vLLM/llama.cpp backend required) | — | — | **yes** (+ layout w/ reading order, LIT) | code Apache-2.0; **weights Open-RAIL-M, commercial use restricted** | yes |
| **openrouter gemini-2.0-flash / gpt-4o-mini vision** (LIT/UNMEASURED) | — | — | — | — | 0 (API) | ~1–3 s/region (LIT) | 0 | no | API terms | no |

\* qwen row is agreement-with-GT where GT is this engine's own owner-accepted output — 100% by construction (circular, disclosed in M0). [TEST RESULT — all measured rows from `m006_bench/results/metrics.json`]

### M2.2 Per-region CER (the complementarity evidence)

| engine | R1 (vertical EN+kana) | R2 | R3 | R4 | R5 | R6 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| vlm proxy glm-5v | 0.016 | 0.045 | 0.000 | **0.000** | 0.061 | 0.006 |
| paddle v5 server | **0.175** | 0.091 | 0.000 | **0.690 (fail)** | 0.076 | 0.070 |
| paddle v4 server | 0.262 | 0.114 | 0.000 | 0.069 | 0.091 | 0.185 |
| easyocr en+ja | 0.286 | 0.364 | 0.167 | 0.345 | 0.333 | 0.134 |
| manga-ocr | 0.968 | 0.465* | ~0.55* | ~0.5* | ~0.5* | ~0.5* (katakana hallucination) |

[TEST RESULT] Key findings F1–F8:

- **F1 (VLM class dominates this workload).** The proxy VLM reads stylized all-caps comic English AND mixed EN+kana at CER 0.016–0.061, where the best local engine per region never beats 0.069 and typically sits at 0.08–0.19. The workload (stylized fonts, mixed script, bubble context) is exactly what VLMs are for; dedicated OCR stacks lose. [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F2 (qwen's 0.0 measured accuracy is credible, not just circular).** The proxy VLM independently reproduces the same texts at CER≈0.02 against the owner-accepted GT — i.e., a second VLM-class model agrees with what the owner accepted, corroborating that the archived GT is a faithful transcription (and incidentally outperforming the GT's own artifacts on R2/R5: `WAS "I"!` / `OTEARAI` vs the GT's `WAS "!!` / `OTEARAH`). [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F3 (v4/v5 complementary per-region wins — the strongest local-chain evidence).** PP-OCRv5 fails R4 outright (0.690, reads `OHM SI1EHHE AAHA SANNNRE`) while PP-OCRv4 reads it at 0.069; v5 beats v4 on the mixed-script R1 (0.175 vs 0.262) and on R2/R6. Neither dominates → an ordered local chain (or per-region best-of) has strictly better coverage than any single local engine. [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F4 (V-3 rotation is REQUIRED for local engines on vertical regions).** Every local engine's best R1 variant is `rot90cw` (the S003 chosen orientation) — upright scores are worse or catastrophic. The registry's adapters must reuse the V-3 ladder for vertical predicates; VLMs handle upright better but still prefer rotation (proxy: upright CER 0.021→rot 0.016; its upright kana reading is slightly weaker). [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F5 (manga-ocr is NOT an EN-comic fallback).** It katakana-hallucinates on Latin text (`ＭＹＮＡＭＥ：ＳＫＯＣＡＮＥ…`) and produced fluent-but-fake Japanese on the T/L note (CER 0.968). Its remit (JP manga, vertical kana) does not intersect this pipeline's EN-source pages except as a possible future JP-kana assist — excluded from the chain proposal. [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F6 (EasyOCR's ja+en mix interferes).** The ja model claims Latin glyph shapes (`ONI ITTE`, `ORRE町T`, `ヅ`) — accuracy 0.27 with the ja model dragging the en model; its virtues are speed (0.36 s/region, fastest warm) and line boxes, but it is dominated by either Paddle row on accuracy. [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F7 (line-box availability is real and free).** Paddle returns `dt_polys` per recognized line (e.g., R3: two lines `[[1,13],[197,13],…]` with scores 0.946/0.990) and EasyOCR returns polygon+conf per line — both engines' outputs are directly consumable by the T2 line-level ordering spec (M4). manga-ocr and the VLMs return text only. [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F8 (resource envelopes).** Local engines: 0.9–2.6 GB RAM, 8.7–23.5 s cold load, 0.36–2.06 s/region warm on CPU — all trivially T4-compatible alongside qwen IF (and only if) the single-resident policy holds, since qwen occupies ~6.7 GB of the T4's 15 GB and detection/CTD/Lama compete for the rest (the B1 E4 ordering precedent). Cloud engines: zero VRAM, network latency only. [MEASURED-LOCAL + LIT]
- PaddlePaddle 3.3.1 CPU + paddleocr 3.7.0 was run with `enable_mkldnn=False` because the oneDNN/PIR static runner hits `ConvertPirAttribute2RuntimeAttribute` in this sandbox (GPU Colab does not take this path; disclosed — does not affect the accuracy measurements). paddleocr 3.7's DEFAULT models are now PP-OCRv6 (auto-downloaded `PP-OCRv6_medium_det/rec`); the v4/v5 rows were pinned explicitly per the directive's "PP-OCRv4/v5" criterion; a v6 row is deferred to the Colab benchmark (M6-B3) where its runner works. [FACT]

## M3 T1 — OCR engine registry + ordered fallback + lazy-load + circuit breaker (DESIGN; no source changes)

### M3.1 CONFIG registry (cell 1, additive)

```python
"ocr": {
    "engine": "qwen",                       # primary (unchanged semantics)
    "engines": {                            # registry — NEW, additive
        "qwen":             {"type": "local_vlm", "model_id": "Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct", "keep_resident": True},
        "paddle_v5":        {"type": "local_ocr", "det": "PP-OCRv5_server_det", "rec": "PP-OCRv5_server_rec", "device": "cpu"},
        "paddle_v4":        {"type": "local_ocr", "det": "PP-OCRv4_server_det", "rec": "PP-OCRv4_server_rec", "device": "cpu"},
        "openrouter_vision":{"type": "cloud_vlm", "model": "google/gemini-2.0-flash-001", "fallback_model": "openai/gpt-4o-mini", "timeout_s": 30},
    },
    "fallback_chain": ["qwen", "openrouter_vision", "paddle_v5", "paddle_v4"],  # ordered; cloud skipped silently if no key
    "engine_reject": {"min_text_len": 2, "reject_markers": ["[OCR_FAILED", "[EMPTY"]},
    "circuit_breaker": {"trips_on": "load_error", "cooldown_pages": 1},        # one failed load attempt per page, not per region
    ...existing keys unchanged...
}
```

`allowed_engines` (the stale literal) becomes derived from `engines.keys()` — closing the M004-flagged non-collapsing-literal defect at the same time. The existing detection precedent (`primary_detector`/`fallback_detector`, cell 1:241–242) and inpainting precedent (`engine`/`fallback_engine`, cell 1:300–301) are the in-repo style to follow. [FACT/design]

### M3.2 ModelManager extension (cell 5, additive methods)

- `ensure_paddle_v5()` / `ensure_paddle_v4()` / `ensure_openrouter_vision()` following the exact `ensure_qwen` shape (instances / load_times / errors / raise-on-load-failure) — the substrate already exists (M1). Cloud engine holds no instance (zero VRAM); a lightweight client object only.
- **Single-resident policy:** `ensure_<engine>(unload_others=True)` for local engines — qwen stays the designated resident (its `keep_resident` reflects that it is also the QA engine and the heaviest reload); local OCR engines run on CPU by default (`device: "cpu"`), so in practice they do NOT evict qwen — the VRAM-hygiene `unload_all(exclude=…)` ordering from B1 (E4) is honored unchanged. This is the directive's "only one local engine resident in VRAM at a time via model_manager; cloud engine = zero VRAM" — implemented by construction (CPU local OCR + cloud client), with the eviction path available for a future GPU-local engine.
- **Circuit breaker:** on load failure, `model_manager.errors[engine]` + a `failed_until_page[engine]` mark — `ocr_page` consults it and skips that engine for the remainder of the page run (fixes the measured 6×-failed-reload pathology per page). A fresh `run_ocr_all(force=True)` resets it.

### M3.3 Engine adapter contract (cell 7 area)

Every registry engine implements `recognize(image_bgr, region, page_id) -> {text, confidence, engine, language_score, warnings, lines?}`:

- **qwen adapter** = the existing `ocr_region` path verbatim (preprocess_for_ocr → V-3 ladder → QwenVLWrapper), `engine: "qwen"`.
- **box-engine adapters (paddle/easyocr/surya-later)** = raw crop (their own internal preprocessing) → V-3 rotation ladder for vertical predicates (F4) → line aggregation with per-line boxes surfaced in `lines` (additive sidecar field, feeds T2 line-level ordering).
- **cloud adapter** = the production prompt (cell 5:351–358) over the OpenRouter chat-completions vision API, base64 crop, timeout + one model fallback; zero VRAM; reads `MANGABD_SECRETS["openrouter_api_key"]` (V-5-proven capture pattern) and self-disables when absent (no error, visible status note).
- **Fallback semantics (the directive's exact rule):** a region retries down `fallback_chain` on (adapter exception OR engine-local reject: empty/short text, reject-markers) and is marked `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` only if ALL chain engines fail — the pipeline never halts on a single engine's death. Each attempt appends to a `fallback_trace` (engine, outcome, ms) kept in the ocr record — the RF-4-visible-report convention extended to engine provenance.
- **Confidence rule (M1 finding):** accept/reject criteria are per-engine (paddle real scores can use a threshold; qwen heuristic only feeds its existing status labels) — never cross-engine comparison.

### M3.4 What stays untouched

Wire format stays additive-only (`ocr.json` records gain optional `engine_used`, `fallback_trace`, `lines`); `build_ocr_row` (cell 7:478–516) gains the new fields; downstream B2 parser, translation, inpaint, render all byte-compatible; `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` semantics (B2/RF-3/RF-4) are the terminal state of the chain, not an intermediate one — so all existing marker handling, visibility and self-tests carry over unchanged.

## M4 T2 — Reading order: spec + MEASURED validation

### M4.1 Region-level algorithm (spec)

1. Input: the page's regions (x, y, w, h, region_type) from `detections.json`.
2. **Column bands** = connected components of x-interval overlap (`[x, x+w]` intervals; union-find, ~15 lines).
3. Bands ordered **right-to-left** (band max_x descending).
4. Within a band, regions ordered **top-to-bottom** (y ascending, x-descending tie-break).
5. Overlay margin notes (`region_type == "overlay"` in the left margin band): placement governed by `CONFIG["reading_order"]["overlay_note_position"] ∈ {geometric, first, last}` — default **geometric** (the validated behavior; the note lands last on the archived page, which matches the GLM-5V-confirmed order).
6. Output: a stable `reading_order` index (0..n-1) written back onto the regions; persisted in `detections.json` (additive per-region field) and as a `translation_df` column.

### M4.2 Validation (archived S003, the directive's known order-sensitive case)

- VLM ground truth, two independent GLM-5V queries: (a) un-annotated page → panel map {right panel: R3; top-left: R2, R4; bottom-left: R5, R6; left-margin note: R1}; (b) red-annotated R1–R6 boxes → **ORDER: R3→R2→R4→R5→R6→R1**. (Query (a)'s free-text order listing was self-inconsistent with its own panel map; the annotated query (b) is the reference — ambiguity resolution disclosed.) [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **Algorithm output: `R3→R2→R4→R5→R6→R1` — EXACT match, Kendall tau +1.000.** [TEST RESULT — `m006_bench/results/reading_order.json`]
- **Current pipeline order (detection-id): `R1→R2→R3→R4→R5→R6` — tau +0.200 vs the confirmed order** (only R2's position coincides; the multi-bubble conversation is misordered for copy payload, translation context, and ai.Text export). The directive's order-defect hypothesis is confirmed and quantified on the archived corpus. [TEST RESULT]
- Consumers to switch (all one-line sorts): `build_ai_text_content` (cell 25:162 `sort_values("id")` → `sort_values(["reading_order","id"])`), `build_translation_records_from_df` (cell 8, same change) — copy payload and ai.Text export delegate to the former (single source of truth, Flash M004 Amendment 4), so the change site count stays minimal; `export_ai_text` (cell 8:1270) inherits via records. S003's archived payload order (note-first) becomes corrected-order; disclosed as an intended visible diff at B2 acceptance.

### M4.3 Line-level algorithm (spec)

- **Horizontal regions** (not V-3 vertical): line boxes sorted by y-center ascending, x ascending tie-break. Validated: on every box-engine region the engine's native emission order ALREADY equals the y-sorted order (nativeCER == ySortedCER on all R2–R6 for both paddles + easyocr) — the sort is a cheap invariant guard, not a correction. [TEST RESULT]
- **Vertical regions** (V-3 predicate `rw<90 ∧ rh>2rw`, cell 7:404): line boxes clustered into columns by x-interval overlap; **columns right-to-left**; within a column top-to-bottom. Degenerate single-column case demonstrated on R1 (paddle: 2–3 line boxes after the V-3 rotation; easyocr: 6). [TEST RESULT]
- **Qwen: prompt-based vs box-based reordering (the directive's evaluation).** Evidence: the proxy VLM (prompt-based, no ordering instruction, same production prompt) returned R6's 13 lines in perfect reading order and R1's column content in correct order after the V-3 rotation — VLM-class models natively emit reading-ordered text for horizontal bubbles and single-column vertical notes. Conclusion: for qwen, **prompt-based ordering is sufficient for B2** (zero new infra); box-based reordering becomes necessary only for multi-column vertical layouts (rare on this workload; no archived instance) — where the Surya reading-order model (layout+order, LIT) is the B3 candidate, or a light line detector + the M4.3 vertical spec. [MEASURED-LOCAL + LIT]
- Line boxes, when the engine supplies them, are stored additively in the ocr record (`lines`) — decision item D4.

## M5 T3 — Failure-modes audit: every dead-engine path + graceful degradation

| # | path (main bytes) | today's behavior on engine death | specified graceful degradation (B1) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ensure_qwen` load raise, called per region (cell 7:389 → cell 5:826–834) | per-region `[OCR_FAILED]` rows; **load re-attempted once per region (6×/page)**; page continues | circuit breaker (1 attempt/page) + chain retry; `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` only after ALL engines fail |
| 2 | `QwenVLWrapper.recognize` inference exception (cell 5:438–449) | marker text row, status `failed`, engine stays "qwen" | chain retry for the region; `fallback_trace` in sidecar |
| 3 | `ocr_page` missing-original raise (cell 7:570–574) | page error recorded, batch continues | keep (not engine-related; correct as-is) |
| 4 | no detections → auto-detect (cell 7:580–588) / empty page (cell 7:590–604) | empty artifact, stage done, continue | keep |
| 5 | `run_ocr_all` per-page catch (cell 15:584–591) | `add_page_error`, next page | keep + engine-level summary line (ok/fallback/failed counts per engine) |
| 6 | UI STEP 1–2 button `on_ocr` (cell 25:351–359) | try/except prints `❌ {e}`; engine death never reaches it (swallowed upstream); user sees marker rows at STEP 3 | visible engine-chain banner ("qwen failed → 6 regions recovered via paddle_v5") + terminal `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` only on chain exhaustion |
| 7 | Run All `run_full_pipeline` (cell 15:694–748) | never halts; manual-engine pause is by design; OCR-failed texts flow to translation as B2-visible skips | same + the B2 skip report gains an `ocr_chain_exhausted` reason breakdown |
| 8 | cell 7 import-time self-tests (cell 7:1112 raise) | cold-start halt of the cell — the only true hard-halt | registry self-test degrades: a broken OPTIONAL engine disables itself (visible) instead of failing the cell; qwen-path self-tests stay mandatory |
| 9 | cloud engine timeout/quota (future `openrouter_vision`) | n/a today | timeout → next chain engine; no-key → silently skipped with status note (V-5 capture pattern) |

[FACT for today's behavior (traced on main bytes); the degradation column is the B1 spec] Note the interaction with RF-4: bare `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` markers are already never applied as translations — the chain's terminal marker inherits that guarantee unchanged.

## M6 Batch plan (proposal — NOTHING below is started; gated on Owner authorization + Flash Phase D per protocol)

- **M6-B1 — engine registry + fallback chain + lazy load + circuit breaker** (cells 1, 5, 7; UI label cell 25). Default chain from the measurements: `qwen → openrouter_vision (if key) → paddle_v5 → paddle_v4` (F1 accuracy ordering; offline subset `qwen → paddle_v5 → paddle_v4` when no key). Gates: m002_sim_v2 0 abort sites; wire format additive-only; 25 cell-8 self-tests untouched; new cell-7 chain self-tests (fake engines, fault injection per M5 rows); Flash Phase D on pushed bytes; Owner live retest (engine-kill scenario: owner deletes/corrupts the qwen cache and watches the chain recover).
- **M6-B2 — reading order** (cell 6/7 compute + df column + consumers cell 8/25). Gates: the M4.2 validation suite re-run on the archived samples (algorithm == VLM-confirmed order; Kendall tau ≥ the Phase-A reference on any new archived page); visible payload-order diff at acceptance; `overlay_note_position` default = geometric (D2).
- **M6-B3 — extra engines / Colab benchmark** ONLY if the Owner judges the justification (per the directive): (a) measure gemini-2.0-flash + gpt-4o-mini on the SAME 6-crop corpus on Colab with the owner key (protocol: `m006_phaseA_bench.py` transplant — same crops, same GT, same metrics; plus PP-OCRv6 default row which needs the GPU/normal runner); (b) evaluate Surya 2 line boxes + reading-order on Colab (vLLM backend) IF its Open-RAIL-M weight license is acceptable (D3); (c) extend the corpus — the current 6-region single-page corpus is honest but SMALL (disclosed limitation; every number above is exactly reproducible, but per-region n=1 for each comic-text style). A 3–5 page corpus with ≥30 regions would make the CER deltas statistically solid; the S001–S007 chain shows the Owner's archiving cadence supports this.
- Suggested sequencing: B1 → B2 (independent, could run as one branch or two), B3 optional after Owner's Colab benchmark.

## M7 Decision items for PM/Owner relay

- **D1** default chain: accuracy-first (qwen → cloud → paddle) vs offline-first (qwen → paddle_v5 → paddle_v4, cloud last)? (measured basis: proxy-VLM 0.021 vs paddle 0.12–0.18.)
- **D2** overlay margin-note placement: geometric (last — validated) vs first? (VLM-confirmed manga order puts the T/L note last; the owner's own reading habit may differ.)
- **D3** Surya weights are Open-RAIL-M (commercial use restricted; code Apache-2.0) — is B3 evaluation still wanted, or exclude on license?
- **D4** store per-line boxes in `ocr.json` (additive schema growth) for T2 line-level ordering — yes/no?
- **D5** keep BOTH paddle v4 and v5 in the chain (complementary wins, F3; ~80 MB extra models, CPU) or one only?
- **D6** circuit breaker scope: per-page (proposed) vs per-session cooldown?
- **D7** `reading_order` stability across re-detection: recompute on every detect (proposed — deterministic) vs freeze once exported?

## §A.1 EXTENDED BENCHMARK — Owner-Requested Engines (GLM-5.3, 2026-10-19)

Directive: PM relay — research AND benchmark 5 Owner-identified engines on the SAME archived crop set (R1–R6) with the SAME methodology, then update this report with the addendum, a revised chain ordering recommendation, a T4 VRAM co-residency check, and a license audit. Docs-only (no source changes), honest tags (MEASURED / LIT / UNMEASURED-VRAM / UNMEASURED-PROXY), commit as "MANGABD-006 Phase A Extension: 5 new engines benchmarked". **All five model IDs verified to exist on HuggingFace before any benchmarking** (API-verified: authors baidu / PaddlePaddle / ATH-MaaS / Kellenok / genshiai-daichi). [FACT]

### A.1.0 Environment, provenance, and instrument parity

- Same corpus, GT, and metrics as M0/M2: the 6 archived crops (R1–R6, shared original md5 `446b3575…`), GT = owner-accepted `translation.json` `original_text`, CER on NFKC+casefold+collapsed, N2 match, per-region + EN-bubble split, R1 rot90cw variant with best-variant selection mirroring the V-3 ladder. `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_metrics.py` re-implements the M0 metric definitions verbatim (same n1/n2/lev/cer/match2 functions). [FACT]
- The sandbox venv was recycled between sessions and rebuilt to match the Phase A environment: **paddlepaddle 3.3.1 CPU + paddleocr 3.7.0** (identical versions to the M2 rows), plus for this extension onnxruntime 1.31.0, and transformers 5.20.1 + torch 2.14.1+cpu + torchvision 0.29.1 (OvisOCR2 route). `enable_mkldnn=False` workaround retained for all paddle rows (M2 note). [FACT]
- Instruments (re-runnable, COMMITTED at RF-5/A8 — filenames corrected from the original e06c045 text, which named a monolithic `m006_phaseA_ext_bench.py` that was never pushed): `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_kellenok.py`, `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_baberu.py`, `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_paddle_v6.py` (both det policies, one process each), `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_metrics.py` (M0 metric definitions verbatim + the C9/A2 audit + the reproduction matrix → `m006_bench/results/metrics_ext.json`); raw per-engine JSONs `m006_bench/results/{kellenok,baberu,paddle_v6}_results.json`. NOT committed, disclosed (A.1.6): the OvisOCR2 runner (transformers CPU route; RF-5 re-run skipped per PM directive) and the `m006_ext/` weights (kellenok det+rec ONNX FP32 23 MB + baberu ONNX build 243 MB, HF-revision-pinned and re-downloaded for the re-measurement; v6 132 MB via the mutable paddleocr auto-download; OvisOCR2 1.71 GB — vendoring = open decision item D12). [FACT]
- **kellenok runner port disclosure:** the runner is an onnxruntime port of the repo's published `examples/onnx_openvino.py` — identical graph, preprocessing (960 long-side / pixel-budget resize, ImageNet norm), DB post-processing (thresh 0.15 / box_thresh 0.25 / unclip 1.4, minAreaRect, min-side 3), rec (h=48 natural width, ±1 norm, CTC greedy, 18,708-symbol dict, vertical crop auto-rotate 90° CCW). Two port notes, disclosed: (a) the reference script's normalization promotes the input to float64 (np.float64 mean/std literals) — OpenVINO silently auto-casts, onnxruntime does not; the first run returned zero detections until an explicit `.astype(np.float32)` was added (harness bug, fixed; not a model defect); (b) the model card's "furigana filter" lives in the author's demo app, not in the published example — not ported, so kellenok's page-level FP count (art-text false positives on the archived page) is an upper bound vs their demo app. [TEST RESULT]

### A.1.1 Research — the five engines (architecture, size, license, strengths, install)

| engine | class / architecture | weights | license | claimed strengths [LIT] | install / run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **baidu/Unlimited-OCR** | VLM doc-parser, DeepseekV2 lineage + DeepEncoder ("pushes DeepSeek-OCR one step further", arXiv 2606.23050, 2026-06); `pipeline_tag: image-text-to-text`, custom code | **6.67 GB** safetensors (single file) + 10 MB tokenizer | **MIT** | one-shot long-horizon document parsing → markdown; multilingual; vLLM support (2026-06-28); gundam 1024/640 crop mode, 32k context | transformers 4.57.1 `trust_remote_code` / vLLM; **NOT runnable in this sandbox (4 GB RAM) → UNMEASURED-VRAM** |
| **PaddlePaddle/PP-OCRv6_medium_det + _medium_rec** | det 15.5 M (LCNetV4 + RepLKFPN + DBHead) + rec 19 M (LCNetV4 + LightSVTR, CTC+NRTR multi-head); 50 languages; arXiv 2606.13108 | det 59.1 MB + rec 72.9 MB inference params | **Apache-2.0** | +5.1% rec accuracy / +4.6% det Hmean vs PP-OCRv5_server (in-house benchmarks); "surpassing billion-scale VLMs"; 2.37× GPU speedup; 3.9× CPU speedup (tiny) | `pip install paddleocr` (3.7 defaults ARE v6) — measured here pinned `PP-OCRv6_medium_det/rec` |
| **ATH-MaaS/OvisOCR2** | 0.8 B end-to-end PAGE doc-parser (post-trained Qwen3.5-0.8B, linear-attention hybrid); `Qwen3_5ForConditionalGeneration`, transformers-native | **1.71 GB** safetensors (bf16) | **Apache-2.0** | OmniDocBench v1.6 overall 96.58 (claimed SOTA, first end-to-end model to top it); PureDocBench 75.06; markdown in "natural reading order" | native: vLLM 0.22.1 (GPU); ALSO loads in transformers 5.x — **MEASURED here on CPU bf16** |
| **Kellenok/PP-OCRv6_manga v0.2** (2026-09-28) | manga-finetuned PP-OCRv6: tiny det (PPLCNetV4+RepLKFPN+DBHead) + small rec (LightSVTR CTC); trained on **Manga109-s + deepghs/AnimeText**; 18,708-symbol dict | det 1.8 MB + rec 21.2 MB ONNX FP32 (FP16: 0.92+10.6 MB) | **Apache-2.0** | author-benchmarked: e2e manga CER 10.03% / webtoon 6.21% / illustration 16.36%; rec comictxt 8.37% (217 western-comic lines); SFX 26.14% | ONNX + onnxruntime only — CPU-first by design; community model (5 likes, 0 downloads at research time) |
| **genshiai-daichi/baberu-ocr v1.1** (2026-10) | 115 M DINOv2 vision + 6-layer GQA char-level decoder (14,630-symbol vocab, no subwords); bubble-crop reader, ja/zh/en | ONNX 243 MB (fp16 vision + int8 decoders) or 122 MB (int4 vision); safetensors 451 MB | **Apache-2.0** | beats manga-ocr on JP (Manga109-v2026 lCER 0.0359 vs 0.0422, independent GT); en per-bubble lCER 0.054 vs PaddleOCR-VL-0.9B 0.081; v1.1 reads to 256 chars; disclosed weakness: vintage western comics lCER 0.1488 | `onnx_infer.py` self-contained (onnxruntime+numpy+pillow) — measured here as-shipped |

### A.1.3 Findings F9–F16

- **F9 (kellenok is the best local engine measured — and the directive's R4 question answers YES).** Kellenok/PP-OCRv6_manga v0.2 scores mean CER **0.031** — better than every local engine in either Phase A table (v4 0.120, v5 0.183, v6 0.244/0.101, EasyOCR 0.271, manga-ocr 0.562), within 1.5 points of the VLM proxy (0.021), at 23 MB of ONNX weights, 0.16 s cold load, 0.35 s/region warm, 384 MB RSS, with line boxes. **On the stylized-font R4 — the exact failure that motivated the multi-engine work — both manga-specialized engines read it PERFECTLY (CER 0.000: kellenok `WHO THE HELL IS THAT PERSON!?`, baberu same) where generic v5 (0.690) and generic v6 medium (0.345/0.379) fail and only generic v4 (0.069) survived.** Manga-specialization (Manga109-s + AnimeText training) directly fixes the stylized-comic-text failure mode of the generic Paddle lineage — the directive's critical test, answered with measurement. [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F10 (baberu: VLM-class EN bubbles at 115 M params; no manga-ocr-style EN hallucination; weak on the tall mixed vertical note).** EN-bubble CER **0.016** — numerically BETTER than the VLM proxy's 0.022 (M2) and far ahead of manga-ocr (0.481 with katakana hallucination on Latin script). The directive's question "does it avoid manga-ocr's English hallucination?" — YES, measured: R3 read exactly, R2/R5 within one character (`"I"!` / `OTEARA!!?`), R6 at 0.006 with the v1.1 256-char headroom (GT 175 chars). R1 mixed vertical note is its weak region (0.325 rot90cw; upright JP-hallucinates exactly like manga-ocr — the F4 "V-3 rotation required" pattern holds): the 224×224 whole-crop input squashes the 61×298 note, garbling the English while still reading the kana `こがねい` correctly. Bubble-level output only (no line boxes). [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F11 (generic PP-OCRv6 medium: official claims verified — but a small-crop det failure the claims don't cover).** The official v6 claims (arXiv 2606.13108 + both HF cards + PaddleOCR docs) are **+5.1% rec accuracy / +4.6% det Hmean vs PP-OCRv5_server** on Baidu's in-house benchmarks; the directive's "+13%" figure appears in NO official source I could find (arxiv, HF cards, PaddleOCR docs/GitHub, deepwiki) — disclosed as not verifiable; the closest official numbers are quoted. Measured on this corpus: v6's default det policy **returns EMPTY on the small R6 crop (131×204: CER 1.000)** — the default `max`-type limit never upscales small crops, and v6-medium's detector misses all 13 lines at native scale (v5 and kellenok's 960-upscale both read it fine; reproduced identically across 660/960/1280 max-side probes). A 960/min upscale policy (sensitivity row) recovers R6 (0.006) and gives the best R1 of ANY local engine (0.032, kana `こがねい` read exactly) but degrades R2 (0.023→0.159) and R4 (0.345→0.379), costs 9.3 s/region and 3.1 GB RSS on CPU. v6's best region-level wins (R1 0.063 default, R2 0.023) do not offset its R4+R6 failures. [MEASURED-LOCAL + LIT]
- **F12 (v6 does NOT displace v4/v5 in the chain — kellenok does).** The directive's complementarity check: v6-default (0.244) and v6-upscale (0.101) both remain worse than v4 (0.120) on the mean and both fail R4, which v4 reads at 0.069. **[AMENDED RF-5/A1 (Flash C5 — the original sentence here claimed "no region where generic v6 is the unique best engine EXCEPT R1-upscale (0.032)", which was defective): R2 ALSO has a unique winner — PP-OCRv6 medium det-default scores R2 CER 0.023 (1/44), beating ALL other engines on R2 (kellenok 0.045, baberu 0.045, VLM-proxy 0.045, v5 0.091, v4 0.114; only v6's own 960/min policy degrades its R2 to 0.159). The chain conclusion survives the correction: the fallback chain retries on FAILURE, not on quality — kellenok's R2 0.045 is a passing read, so v6's R2 margin is a quality edge, not a coverage gap, and v6-default still fails R4 (0.345) and R6 (1.000, empty det) either way.]** Generic v6's unique-best regions are therefore R2-default (0.023) and R1-upscale (0.032) — niches already covered at chain level by kellenok (R2 0.045 passing, R1 0.103) and the VLM engines (R1 0.016–0.087). Meanwhile kellenok **strictly dominates v4 on this corpus** (wins or ties every region: R1 0.103 vs 0.262, R2 0.045 vs 0.114, R4 0.000 vs 0.069, R5 0.030 vs 0.091, R6 0.006 vs 0.185) — no v4/v5/kellenok complementarity remains; kellenok replaces the paddle rows in the default chain (v4 demoted to optional last-resort or dropped, D10). [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F13 (OvisOCR2: measured, and it is a page doc-parser, not a crop-OCR fallback).** Loaded and run locally via transformers 5.20.1 CPU bf16 (1.71 GB weights, 2.4 GB RSS — fits this 4 GB sandbox, answering the directive's "if VRAM permits" with YES for CPU). Raw result with the production OCR prompt: **CER 1.000 on every region** — unguarded greedy decoding repetition-loops (e.g. R3: `MY NAME'S KOGANEI YOU DUMBASS! DUMBASS! DUMBASS!…` to the 512-token cap; R2: the sentence repeated 4×) and emits a hallucinated output-filename artifact (`3a46a174.md`). The model card itself ships a repetition-tail cleaner for its vLLM route — the pathology is known to the authors. With a disclosed decode-guard diagnostic (strip filename artifacts/`</think`, collapse consecutive duplicate sentence-units) the core readings emerge: **0.195 mean** — R1 0.087 (kana exact, EN one-char slips), R6 0.057, but R2 0.227 / R3 0.267 / R4 0.379 (sentence-level loops the simple guard cannot collapse; genuine misreadings on stylized R4). This is NOT the VLM-proxy class (0.021) — it is a 0.8B page-level document parser being asked a crop-level OCR task; its actual product is full-page markdown. **Page-level T2 evidence (one run, native doc-parse prompt):** it transcribed all 6 regions in `R1→R2→R4→R3→R5→R6` order — **LTR document order, NOT the confirmed manga RTL order** (`R3→R2→R4→R5→R6→R1`, M4.2) — so a doc-parser VLM cannot replace the M4.1 region-level RTL algorithm for manga. Deployment verdict: excluded from the OCR fallback chain; a possible future page-parser adjunct only, GPU (vLLM) route, with its shipped repetition guards. [MEASURED-LOCAL]
- **F14 (Unlimited-OCR: UNMEASURED-VRAM, research-only row).** 6.67 GB safetensors cannot load in the 4 GB sandbox — honest tag, no benchmark. Research findings: DeepSeek-OCR-successor VLM doc-parser (DeepseekV2 + DeepEncoder, arXiv 2606.23050, Baidu, MIT license — the permissive license is notable for a frontier-class model); one-shot long-horizon document parsing to markdown, multilingual; vLLM backend since 2026-06-28; transformers 4.57.1 + trust_remote_code on GPU. Same task class as OvisOCR2 (page-level document parsing) — by extrapolation from F13 and its architecture, it is NOT a candidate for the per-crop comic-OCR fallback chain; T4 feasibility is swap-class (below). Colab protocol if the Owner wants the number: `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_ovis2.py` transplant with vLLM/transformers GPU route, same 6 crops + page, same metrics (that runner was never committed — RF-5/A8 scope boundary, see A.1.6). [UNMEASURED-VRAM + LIT]
- **F15 (license audit — all five clean).** baidu/Unlimited-OCR: **MIT** (LICENSE file 1,061 B, MIT text). PP-OCRv6 det+rec: **Apache-2.0**. OvisOCR2: **Apache-2.0** (11,544 B LICENSE). Kellenok/PP-OCRv6_manga: **Apache-2.0**. baberu-ocr: **Apache-2.0**. **No Open-RAIL-M, no commercial restrictions, nothing that blocks production use among the five.** The only Open-RAIL-M item in the entire Phase A record remains Surya's WEIGHTS (M2.1 row, D3 — unchanged). Two diligence notes for the two community models (kellenok: 0 downloads at research time; baberu: 943): licenses are clean and weights are small and pin-able by revision sha — supply-chain risk is low and mitigable by vendoring the weights (D12). [FACT — license fields verified against each repo's card + LICENSE file via HF API]
- **F16 (resource envelopes + T4 16 GB co-residency with lazy-load discipline — the directive's Q5).** Measured here (CPU): kellenok 384 MB RSS / 0.16 s cold; baberu 624 MB / 1.12 s; v6 1.6–3.1 GB / 7–9 s; ovis2 2.4 GB / 10 s. On the Colab T4 (15 GB usable), with the M3.2 design (local OCR engines on `device: cpu`, cloud zero-VRAM): **{Qwen ~6.7 GB + PP-OCRv6 (CPU) + baberu (CPU/ONNX) + kellenok (CPU/ONNX)} co-reside trivially — the OCR engines consume ZERO VRAM by construction**, leaving qwen + CTD + LaMa the full 15 GB (the B1 E4 ordering unchanged). **OvisOCR2** (1.71 GB bf16 weights → ~2.5–3.5 GB VRAM with activations) would fit numerically alongside qwen (≈10 GB total) but violates the single-resident-VLM discipline — it is **swap-class** (evict qwen, run, reload). **Unlimited-OCR** (6.67 GB weights → ~7–9 GB with KV/activations at 32k ctx) does NOT fit alongside qwen — swap-class only, or run on a bigger GPU/cloud. The practical chain answer: keep qwen resident + kellenok/baberu CPU-resident (their 0.16–1.1 s cold loads make even per-batch lazy reloads free); treat both large VLMs as page-parser adjuncts behind explicit swaps, never as co-resident fallbacks. [MEASURED-LOCAL + LIT-derived]

### A.1.4 Revised chain ordering recommendation (the directive's Q4, evidence-based)

**Recommended default chain (revising M3.1/M6-B1): `qwen → openrouter_vision (if key) → kellenok → baberu → [paddle_v4 optional last-resort]`; offline subset `qwen → kellenok → baberu → [paddle_v4]`.**

- kellenok displaces BOTH paddle rows as the primary local fallback (F9/F12: strictly dominates v4, beats v5 everywhere, line boxes, 23 MB, onnxruntime-only — no paddlepaddle dependency in the notebook at all, a deployment simplification worth noting).
- baberu enters as the next local step (F10: EN-bubble 0.016 ties the VLM class; 115 M; complementary shape — bubble-level, no boxes, weak on tall mixed vertical notes — its failures (R1 0.325) are covered upstream by kellenok (R1 0.103)).
- PP-OCRv4 demoted to optional last-resort (D10): its only unique virtue left is generic-document robustness outside manga (in-domain evidence says kellenok covers this corpus strictly better).
- PP-OCRv5 and generic PP-OCRv6-medium: NOT in the chain (v5 strictly dominated; v6 fails R4 + small-crop det by default — F11/F12). OvisOCR2 and Unlimited-OCR: excluded (F13/F14 — doc-parser class, swap-class VRAM).
- Registry delta vs M3.1 (same additive shape): `"kellenok": {"type": "local_ocr", "backend": "onnxruntime", "det": "manga_det_v0.2.onnx", "rec": "manga_rec_v0.2.onnx", "dict": "ppocrv6_dict.txt", "device": "cpu"}` and `"baberu": {"type": "local_ocr", "backend": "onnx", "vision": "vision_fp16.onnx", "decoders": "int8", "device": "cpu"}`; `fallback_chain` updated accordingly; adapter contract unchanged (M3.3) — both are box/bubble engines reusing the V-3 rotation ladder for vertical predicates (F4 holds for both: baberu upright JP-hallucinates, kellenok upright fragments — rot90cw is the best variant for both on R1, exactly as measured).

### A.1.5 Batch plan + decision-item updates

- **M6-B1** (unchanged shape, new engines): registry + fallback chain + lazy load + circuit breaker, now with kellenok + baberu as the local chain members per A.1.4. ONNX engines add one new dependency (onnxruntime) and two vendored weight sets (~266 MB) — Colab/Drive vendoring per D12.
- **M6-B2** unchanged (reading order; kellenok's line boxes strengthen the line-level ordering story — 40 page-level boxes measured on the archived page).
- **M6-B3** (revised): the original Colab items stand (gemini-2.0-flash + gpt-4o-mini with the Owner key; Surya behind D3; PP-OCRv6-on-GPU deprioritized — measured here as not chain-relevant); NEW optional items per this extension: (a) OvisOCR2 on T4 via vLLM with its shipped repetition guards (page-parser adjunct evaluation), (b) Unlimited-OCR on T4 swap-class (doc-parser adjunct), both only if the Owner sees value in page-level markdown export for a future feature.
- **New decision items D8–D12 (appended to M7):**
  - **D8** adopt kellenok as the primary local fallback engine (replacing v5/v4 in the default chain)?
  - **D9** baberu's position: full chain member (4th) vs EN-bubble specialist invoked selectively?
  - **D10** retain PP-OCRv4 as optional last-resort, or drop the paddle dependency entirely?
  - **D11** if any v6-family engine is kept at all: adopt the 960/min det upscale policy for small crops (F11) in its adapter?
  - **D12** vendor the two community-model weights (kellenok 23 MB + baberu 243/122 MB) into the repo/Drive pinned by revision sha (supply-chain hygiene, F15)?

### A.1.6 RF-5 — Flash ext-review binding amendments (A1/A2/A8), closed 2026-10-19

**Review verdict under which this record was amended:** Flash independent review of §A.1 at `e06c045`, delivered as commit `96839a6` on branch `mangabd-006-flash-ext-review` (report-only, cut from `main @ e06c045`, no source/sample changes): **ACCEPT WITH AMENDMENTS** — P0 scope/traceability (docs-only confirmed, but the claimed instruments were in NEITHER the pushed tree NOR the reviewer sandbox → every MEASURED row carried UNVERIFIED-BYTES-UNAVAILABLE; minimal closure ask = commit the raw JSONs + ext scripts), P2 C5 (the F12 no-unique-best sentence defective → A1), P2 C9 (kellenok vs baberu R2–R6 identical CERs — aggregation-copy bug and innocent shared-GT-artifact explanations both consistent; must resolve BEFORE the M6-B1 registry freeze → A2). Binding items A1 + A2 + A8; chain direction + D8–D12 endorsed. All three closed by the RF-5 commit on `main` (this commit). [FACT]

**A2 — C9 investigation result: COINCIDENCE (GT artifacts + equal edit distances), not a bug.** The original 2026-10-18 raw outputs were lost to the sandbox recycle, so the anomaly was resolved by deterministic re-measurement of BOTH engines on re-downloaded sha-pinned weights (the A8 closure run; instruments and JSONs listed below, audit block in `metrics_ext.json`): the re-measured kellenok/baberu comparison confirms **5/6 identical CERs but only 4/6 byte-identical predictions** — the five EN-region CER identity (R2–R6: 0.045 / 0.000 / 0.000 / 0.030 / 0.006) is REAL, not copied. Mechanism: (i) R3–R6 predictions are byte-identical between the two engines because both read the same text off the image — R3/R4 GT-verbatim, and R5/R6 the true image text where the owner-accepted GT itself carries qwen artifacts, so a CORRECT read lands at a small nonzero distance (R5: 2/66 — the SFX-cluster glyph + one `!` of the `!!?` tail; R6: 1/157 — the GT's `SUDENLY` typo vs the true `SUDDENLY`); (ii) **R2 predictions DIFFER while both sit at exactly 2/44 = 0.045** — kellenok reads the GT-artifact tail as `WAS "T'!`, baberu reads the true text `WAS "I"!`, and baberu's R2 string is byte-identical (at the N1 metric level) to the independent glm-5v-turbo VLM-proxy R2 — a third, architecturally unrelated engine — which an aggregation-copy bug between the two ONNX runners could not produce; (iii) R1 differs in both CER (0.103-class vs 0.325) and string, as expected for two different architectures on the tall mixed note. **Conclusion: the identical CERs arise from equal edit distances against a shared GT-artifact set — coincidence, not a copied-value defect; the C9 blocker on the M6-B1 registry freeze is discharged.** [TEST RESULT — re-measured 2026-10-19, re-runnable via `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_metrics.py` (audit block); VLM-proxy R2 from the Phase A `vlm_R2.json` workspace artifact]

**A8 — instruments + raw per-engine JSONs committed (this commit), with provenance.** Rather than reconstruct the lost 2026-10-18 outputs from memory, the closure re-MEASURED all three committable engines deterministically and commits the real bytes: `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_kellenok.py` (onnxruntime port of the model repo's `examples/onnx_openvino.py`; the A.1.0 port disclosure applies, plus two further deviations disclosed in the script and its result JSON — the 960 long-side det normalization for small/rotated ladder crops, and the reading-order sort for joined line text), `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_baberu.py` (the shipped `onnx_infer.py` inference class embedded verbatim, measured as-shipped), `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_paddle_v6.py` (paddleocr 3.7 with `PP-OCRv6_medium_det/rec` pinned, M2 workarounds retained, BOTH det policies — default and 960/min — one process each), and `scripts/m006_phaseA_ext_metrics.py` (M0 metric definitions verbatim + the C9/A2 audit + the reproduction matrix) → `m006_bench/results/kellenok_results.json`, `baberu_results.json`, `paddle_v6_results.json`, `metrics_ext.json`. Crops + GT derive from repository bytes (`samples/S003_batch2` `original.jpg` + `detections.json` + `translation.json`; byte-verified against the archived workspace crops 6/6). Weight provenance: kellenok + baberu re-downloaded from HuggingFace pinned by revision sha with file sha256s recorded in each JSON (kellenok `b3b65cbd…`, baberu `fdf292a1…`); v6 official model files re-fetched by paddleocr auto-download (NOT revision-pinned — relevant to the deltas below). **Cell-level reproduction vs the published A.1.2 table (the published cells stand as the original run's record; deltas disclosed, never silently substituted):** baberu **7/7 cells** (fully deterministic — R1 0.325 rot90cw, R2 0.045 `"I"!`, R3/R4 0.000, R5 0.030 `OTEARA!!?`, R6 0.006); kellenok **5/7** — R1 re-measured 0.063 (8/126) vs 0.103 (13/126) recorded: same rot90cw-best variant, kana `こがねい` read exactly, same error class (quote/space/vowel slips), the original runner's exact R1-ladder det detail not being pinned by the A.1.0 disclosure is the disclosed root-cause candidate; R2–R6 reproduce EXACTLY (2/44, 0, 0, 2/66, 1/157). paddle_v6 **5/7** and paddle_v6_up **4/7** — the non-reproducing cells are v6-R4 (published 0.345/0.379 vs re-measured 0.000/0.000: v6 medium reads the stylized R4 GT-exact under every faithful configuration probed — det policies default/min-960, orientation modules on/off) and v6-up-R2 (published 0.159 vs re-measured 0.045). Root-cause candidates, disclosed not adjudicated: the paddle `~/.paddlex/official_models` cache is mutable and un-pinned (unlike the HF-revision-pinned ONNX engines — the 2026-10-18 and 2026-10-19 fetches are not provably the same bytes), and the original v6 runner's exact module flags are not pinned by the published record; the impact is bounded — v6's chain exclusion (F11/F12, D8–D11) rests on its DEFAULT-policy R6 empty-det failure (REPRODUCED: R6 1.000, 0 lines) and resource envelope (REPRODUCED: 960/min at 9.5 s/region and 3.09 GB RSS), and the re-measured resource envelopes match the published class throughout (kellenok 0.35 s/region warm / 366 MB-class RSS, baberu 1.2 s / 623 MB, v6 default 1.1 s/region). Full matrix in `metrics_ext.json` `reproduction`. [TEST RESULT]

**A8 scope boundaries (disclosed):** the OvisOCR2 runner + raw JSON are NOT committed — the RF-5 ovis re-run was skipped per PM directive; its verdict is already established by the §A.1 measurements and the model card's own repetition-pathology disclaimer (raw greedy 1.000 with repetition loops, decode-guarded 0.195, 88 s/region CPU, page markdown in LTR document order — not manga RTL, F13), and its MEASURED rows keep the UNVERIFIED-BYTES-UNAVAILABLE qualifier in downstream gates (Flash P6). baidu/Unlimited-OCR remains UNMEASURED-VRAM (no JSON exists to commit). The `m006_ext/` weights are NOT vendored into the repo — the kellenok (23 MB) + baberu (243 MB) weight sets are HF-revision-pinned and re-downloadable by the committed scripts; vendoring stays an open decision item (D12). [FACT]

# MANGABD-006 — PHASE C (BATCH 1): OCR ENGINE REGISTRY + FALLBACK CHAIN + LAZY-LOAD + MANUAL SELECTION + WEIGHT VENDORING (GLM-5.3, 2026-10-19)

## PC1-C1 Implementation (per cell)

**Cell 5 — registry + ONNX wrappers + ModelManager lazy-load (3 patches).**
(a) Section 3b: `MANGABD_CONFIG["ocr"]` registry by **additive merge** — `engine: "auto"` (mode semantics: auto = fallback chain, or one registry name = manual), `fallback_chain: [qwen, openrouter_vision, kellenok, baberu]` (the RF-5-endorsed revised chain, paddle optional-slot dropped per the directive's exact list), `engines` registry with the directive's per-engine fields (type/model/weights_path/vrmb_gb/load_fn/unload_fn/key_env) — **all existing cell-1 ocr keys preserved** (qwen_model_id, deterministic, do_sample, num_beams, max_new_tokens, thresholds, preprocess — runtime-proven by cell-5 Test 7 passing on the merged config). `allowed_engines` re-derived from registry keys (the M004-flagged stale non-collapsing `["qwen","ovis","hybrid"]` literal had zero consumers — defect closed per §M3.1). Visible registry banner + onnxruntime availability probe (missing → visible note, engines self-disable through the chain — M5 row 8).
(b) Section 4b: `KellenokOnnxOCR` (the committed RF-5 runner's proven port — det 960 long-side normalization with the disclosed downscale-only budget branch, DB post-processing thresh 0.15/box 0.25/unclip 1.4, h=48 rec, CTC greedy, vertical line auto-rotate; A.1.0 port notes carried in the class docstring; + `read_text` adapter entry) and `BaberuOnnxOCR` (shipped `onnx_infer.py` verbatim decode — greedy + rep-penalty 1.2 + run caps 12/16, max_new_tokens 256; vision default = the **vendored int4 build**; + `read_text`). Both use **lazy imports** (`onnxruntime`/`pyclipper` inside `__init__` with actionable install hints) so a missing package disables the ENGINE via the chain's load-error path, never the cell.
(c) ModelManager extension: `ensure_kellenok`/`ensure_baberu` (vendored-weights resolution: CWD-relative `models/<engine>` → `MODELS_DIR/<engine>`; missing → visible error listing both locations → raise → chain continues), `ensure_openrouter_vision` (lightweight client dict, zero VRAM, key read at call time per the V-5 pattern), four `unload_*` registry wrappers, and the dispatch pair **`ensure_ocr_engine(name, force)` / `unload_ocr_engine(name)`**: registry-validated, early-return when resident, and the directive's lazy-load discipline — `unload_all(exclude=[engine])` before every cold LOCAL load (only one local OCR engine resident at a time), cloud loads evict nothing; dispatch via the registry's `load_fn`/`unload_fn` strings.

**Cell 7 — adapters + fallback chain + Tests 26–28 (11 patches).**
Section 5b: `_OCR_REJECT_MARKERS` + `_ocr_chain_result_ok` (success = non-empty text with no reject marker — see disclosure D-2), `_crop_for_ocr_engine` (ocr_region's crop block verbatim semantics), `_is_vertical_ocr_region` (the V-3 predicate on REGION geometry), `_ocr_result_from_text` (adapter tail: cell-5 heuristic confidence + language score + status labels — per-engine criteria, never cross-engine), `_run_vertical_ladder` (V-3 rotated-only candidates, strict-`>` tie-break from ROTATE_90_CLOCKWISE — ocr_region's exact semantics; the A.1.2-measured best variants align: kellenok/baberu R1 = rot90cw), `_ocr_region_onnx` (raw crop → engine's own preprocessing), `_openrouter_vision_read` (single vision chat-completions call: production `default_prompt`, temperature 0 + max_tokens 512 mirroring qwen's deterministic config, timeout 30 s, the cell-8 translate provider's client pattern + endpoint), `_ocr_region_openrouter` (V-5 key capture `MANGABD_SECRETS` → env fallback; no key → clean RuntimeError → visible chain skip; preprocess_for_ocr + V-3 ladder — a VLM like qwen), **`run_ocr_engine`** (dispatch: qwen → existing `ocr_region` VERBATIM per §M3.3; kellenok/baberu → ONNX adapters; openrouter_vision → cloud; unknown → ValueError), **`ocr_region_with_fallback`** (auto = ordered chain with per-attempt trace; manual = single engine, break on first failure; `[EMPTY]` precheck before the chain; recovery banner `🔁 OCR fallback` when a non-first engine wins; chain-exhausted → terminal `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` with the full trace in warnings; unknown manual mode → visible ValueError, never a silent skip). `ocr_page` gains `engine_mode` (None → CONFIG resolve), per-region call now goes through the chain, `engine_counts` per page + stage meta `ocr_engine_mode`/`ocr_engine_counts` + the §M5-row-5 chain summary line; the belt-and-braces except-path engine label `"qwen"` → `"failed"` (chain semantics — disclosure D-5); `ocr_all_pages` pass-through. Tests 26 (registry shape + loader wiring, incl. all four load_fn/unload_fn methods existing), 27 (auto recovery on BOTH reject and error paths via fake engines with save/restore in `finally` — RF-2/RF-4 inert-test conventions; manual terminal-skip with exactly one attempt; trace outcomes + keys), 28 (guards: unknown engine in ensure/manual-mode/run-dispatch all raise ValueError; non-resident unload no-op; Test-27 restoration proof; cell-7 wiring contract via inspect).

Vendored into the repo (`models/`, committed — not gitignore'd):
- **kellenok** (`models/kellenok/`): `det/manga_det_v0.2.onnx` + `rec/manga_rec_v0.2.onnx` + `ppocrv6_dict.txt`, **23.1 MB, EXACTLY the RF-5 sha-pinned A.1.2-measured files** (`d132078c…`, `de12c84c…`, `b5f2bfe2…`; hub rev `b3b65cbd…`) — zero provenance deviation.

Total 122.3 MB — the directive's "~115 MB" class.

## PC1-C3 Interpretation disclosures + known-opens for Flash Phase D

- **D-1 Registry merge, not replace.** The directive's sketch shows `MANGABD_CONFIG["ocr"] = {…}`; implemented as an additive per-key merge in cell 5 because cell-1's existing keys are load-bearing (QwenVLWrapper decode params, cell-5 Test 7, thresholds) — replacing would delete them. `engine`'s value changes `"qwen"` → `"auto"` (mode semantics), disclosed as the one value-level change; the qwen path under auto is behavior-identical to before (chain head, resident-early-return, ocr_region verbatim).
- **D-2 Success predicate.** Directive-literal check extended from `result.get("text")` to "non-empty AND no reject marker": `QwenVLWrapper.recognize` CATCHES inference exceptions and returns marker text (`[OCR_FAILED: …]`) — without the marker exclusion the chain would accept a failed qwen read as success. `min_text_len` (§M3.1) and the repetition-ratio reject (ext-review A7) are NOT implemented — non-binding follow-ups; a genuine 1-char read therefore still counts as success.
- **D-3 `openrouter_vision` registry entry gains `load_fn`/`unload_fn`** beyond the directive's 4-key sketch — required for uniform registry-driven dispatch (the `ensure_ocr_engine` guard would otherwise reject the cloud engine); the directive's own load_fn/unload_fn convention applied to all four engines. The "no unload needed" directive sentence is honored as behavior (cloud load evicts nothing).
- **D-4 Chain quality semantics.** The chain retries on FAILURE only — `low_confidence`/`non_english` statuses are NOT chain failures (the repeatedly-adjudicated F12 principle); quality flows to STEP 3 exactly as today.
- **D-5 `ocr_page` except-path engine label** `"qwen"` → `"failed"` (that belt-and-braces path fires only on non-engine errors; no consumer branches on the old literal — verified).
- **D-6 `[EMPTY]` precheck** duplicated into the chain wrapper so a too-small crop keeps today's exact `[EMPTY]`/status-empty behavior instead of burning the chain into `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` (M5 row 4: region property ≠ engine failure).
- **D-7 onnxruntime is NOT added to cell 0** (out of scope {5,7,15,25}); the engines self-disable visibly via the chain on import failure (probe note at cell-5 build + per-load error). pyclipper is already in cell 0's DETECTION_PACKAGES. Owner one-liner for full local-chain capability: `!pip install onnxruntime`.
- **D-8 Single-resident rule is directive-literal** (`unload_all(exclude=[engine])` before a cold local OCR load): a cold OCR load also evicts resident NON-OCR models (rtdetr/ctd/lama) — strictly better VRAM hygiene, consistent with the B1-E4 unload→ensure ordering, disclosed as a behavior change vs. today's co-residency (steady-state is unaffected: an already-resident engine early-returns without evicting anything).
- **D-9 `vrmb_gb` values are directive-verbatim metadata** (qwen 6.7 / openrouter 0 / kellenok 0.4 / baberu 0.2); measured RSS context: kellenok ≈ 0.4 GB class ✓, baberu fp16 623 MB (int4 build smaller) vs the declared 0.2 envelope — CPU-resident either way (zero VRAM by construction); no functional consumer of the field in B1.
- **D-10 Cloud adapter determinism choices:** temperature 0 + max_tokens 512 (mirrors the qwen deterministic config; the directive specifies the model but not decode params), timeout 30 s (§M3.3), no cloud-timeout circuit breaker (ext-review A6 — non-binding), no `fallback_model` (the §M3.1 sketch's gpt-4o-mini fallback is not in the directive's registry and stays a follow-up).
- **D-11 Tests 26–28 placement** in cell 7 (M6 plan's "cell-7 chain self-tests") with global numbering continued from cell 8 — the cell-8 battery byte-untouched (see PC1-C0).
- **D-12 Vendored baberu ≠ the A.1.2-measured fp16 build** at the vision-tower bytes (see PC1-C1V for the full evidence: chain-bearing rows identical, R1 better, EN-bubble CER equal); kellenok vendored = the measured bytes exactly. The A.1.2 table rows STAND as the fp16 record; the vendored-int4 re-measurement is committed as a separate artifact for Flash cross-check.
- **D-13 Harness disclosure:** the cell-5 sandbox exec's single-substring `/root` cache-path redirect (see V3) — Colab-root convention vs. sandbox user; asserted one-substring; repo bytes proven against git independently.
- **Known-opens (non-blocking):** min_text_len / repetition-ratio rejects (D-2); A6 cloud-timeout breaker; `fallback_model` (D-10); `allowed_engines` now derived but still unconsumed (kept for future UI validation); a future cell-0-authorized batch could add onnxruntime to the install list (D-7); `run_ocr_all`'s banner resolves the mode once per batch (per-region re-resolution not needed — the dropdown updates CONFIG between runs, not mid-run).

Instruments (session workspace, re-runnable): `scripts/m006_b1_download_weights.py` (sha-pinned vendoring), `scripts/m006_b1_baberu_int4_remeasure.py` + `m006_b1_baberu_int4_results.json` (vendored-build provenance), `scripts/m006_b1_content5.py`/`content7.py`/`content7b.py` (patch texts), `scripts/m006_b1_build.py` (22 anchored patches + manifest `m006_b1_patches.json`), `scripts/m006_b1_verify.py` (99 checks), `scripts/m006_b1_extract_cells.py`, `cells_m006_b1/` (base extraction) + `cells_m006_b1_post/` (post-build extraction). ## PC1-C4 RF-7: weights self-heal (Owner Colab live-test fix) + RF-6 instrument fold-in (GLM-5.3, 2026-10-20)

Directive: PM relay — the Owner live-tested `mangabd-006-b1` in Colab (session **20261019_192**). The new dropdown + manual selection + graceful-failure design **WORKED** (visible trace, no crash, `[SKIP:OCR_FAILED]` on all 6 regions when the engine failed), but kellenok/baberu failed with **"weights পাওয়া যায়নি"** — the Owner's Colab had only the notebook file, not the repo's vendored `models/` folder, and no Drive copy. Fix on `mangabd-006-b1`, cell-5 loader (+ self-test 29 in cell 7); fold in the pending RF-6 artifact-commit (the Phase D P2 recommendation). [FACT]

**Design — three-tier self-heal (directive-literal):**
1. **Vendored present** (repo-relative `models/<engine>/` CWD-relative, or the documented Drive path `MODELS_DIR/<engine>` — on Drive-mounted Colab that is `/content/drive/MyDrive/MangaBD_V12/models/<engine>`): sha256-verify every file against the embedded pins → use. **Zero network; behavior with vendored bytes present is byte-for-byte unchanged** (proven at runtime: identical stdout incl. the M6-B1 load banner, no download banner, and the kellenok R3 read reproduces the committed RF-5 measurement byte-exactly through the new path).
2. **Missing** → auto-download from the HF hub **at the sha-pinned revisions** — kellenok `b3b65cbd…` (3 files, 23.1 MB), baberu `fdf292a1…` int4 bundle (5 files, 122.3 MB; one-time ~145 MB total) — saved under `models/<engine>/`, **every file sha256-verified, then loaded**; subsequent runs find the local bundle and stay offline.
3. **Failure** (any tier: download exception, missing file, sha mismatch on vendored OR downloaded bytes) → **refusal + expanded Bengali diagnostic**: what was tried (candidate paths + the pinned repo@rev attempt with the file count), the root cause, and three remedies — (a) the exact `git clone https://github.com/iskultri-scans/MangaBD-.git` one-liner, (b) the Drive path alternative (copy `models/<engine>/` to `MODELS_DIR/<engine>`), (c) delete a partial/corrupt file and re-run (the download path retries by itself).

**Implementation (cells {5, 7} only):**
- **Cell 5:** (a) registry engine specs gain the provenance pins `hf_repo` / `hf_revision` / `files{relpath → sha256, size}` — the exact committed `scripts/m006_b1_weights_manifest.json` values (RF-5 A8 pins + the M6-B1 int4 pin); (b) module-level `_sha256_of_file` (1 MB chunk streaming — no memory blowup on the 122 MB vision file); (c) `ModelManager` gains `_engine_weights_candidates` (the two documented locations, `_resolve_vendored_engine_dir`'s order), `_download_engine_file` (the downloader: lazy `huggingface_hub.hf_hub_download` at the pinned revision + copy into place — the single monkey-patch surface), `_verify_engine_weights` (per-file pin check → refusal RuntimeError naming the file + expected/got sha prefixes), `_engine_weights_diagnostic` (the expanded Bengali message builder), and **`_ensure_engine_weights`** (the three-tier flow; target-dir rule: the first candidate dir that exists is completed in place — a half-copied Drive bundle heals itself — else a fresh `MODELS_DIR/<engine>`); (d) `ensure_kellenok`/`ensure_baberu` call `_ensure_engine_weights` instead of `_resolve_vendored_engine_dir` — **each function otherwise byte-identical** (verified as an exact one-line delta vs `d569c08`); `_resolve_vendored_engine_dir` kept byte-identical as the legacy path for pin-less engines (no removals).
- **Cell 7:** **Test 29** in the Tests 26-28 block — (ক) REAL pins + missing weights: the monkey-patched downloader is invoked exactly once per pinned file with the pinned repo+revision+filename (kellenok 3 @ `b3b65cbd…`, baberu 5 @ `fdf292a1…`), and deliberately wrong bytes → sha refusal whose message carries "sha mismatch" + the git-clone remedy; (খ) fake pins + missing weights: download → verify → use (files land in the target dir with matching sha); (গ) vendored-valid: verified + used with ZERO downloader calls (zero network); (ঘ) vendored-corrupt: refusal, zero network. All monkey-patching (candidates + downloader + the kellenok spec pins) save/restored in `finally`; hermetic per-phase tmp dirs; NO real network anywhere in the test.

**Verification — 64/64 ALL GREEN (`scripts/m006_b1_rf7_verify.py`):**
- **V0** 8/8 vendored-file sha256 (unchanged bytes).
- **V1 static:** AST 27/27 warnings-as-errors; **25 non-target cells byte-identical to `d569c08`** (RF-7 touches exactly {5, 7}); re-derivation equality (post == `d569c08` + exactly the 9 anchored patches, manifest `scripts/m006_b1_rf7_patches.json`); registry text carries every manifest revision + sha exactly once; `_resolve_vendored_engine_dir` byte-identical; `ensure_kellenok`/`ensure_baberu` differ from `d569c08` by exactly the one call-site line; wiring marker counts; Test-29 presence + hermeticity markers; cell-8 byte-untouched.
- **V2** non-source + top-level notebook fields identical.
- **V3 runtime harness** (cells 5 → 7 → 8, the M6-B1 mocked-globals harness + the same disclosed single-substring `/root` cache redirect): cell 5 battery **9/9**; cell 7 battery **10/10** (6 original + Tests 26-29, zero ❌); cell 8 **25/25**; runtime registry pins == provenance manifest (repo+rev+sha+size); Test 29 leaves the real pins restored.
- **V4 vendored-present behavior:** kellenok manual R3 read through the full new path = **BYTE-EXACT vs the committed RF-5 measurement**; stdout identical to M6-B1 (load banner present, no download banner); baberu int4 manual R3 read via the new path.
- **V5 self-heal behavior:** vendored-present → downloader NEVER called (assert-raise trap); **REAL sha-pinned HF downloads re-executed** — kellenok 3/3 + baberu tokenizer pair 2/2, every sha == pin (the pinned revisions still resolve on the hub today; kellenok downloaded in full, baberu's ONNX trio not re-fetched in-harness — E3 already live-verified that hub tree and the code path is identical per-file); full flow on a hermetic empty dir → download → verify → return dir; sha-mismatch refusal + diagnostic; network-down → expanded diagnostic (cause carried, remedies present); corrupt VENDORED bundle → refusal with no re-download.

[FACT]

**Interpretation disclosures for the Flash delta check:**
- **D-21 Vendored-present strictness upgrade (disclosed behavior delta):** with vendored bytes present the load is stdout-identical and zero-network (proven), but a cold load now ALSO silently sha-verifies the bundle (kellenok ≈ 23 MB / baberu ≈ 122 MB streaming ≈ 0.1–1 s) and a previously-loading-but-corrupt bundle that would have failed inside onnxruntime with a cryptic error now refuses EARLY with the full diagnostic — strictly better failure mode, disclosed as the one vendored-path change. A COMPLETE valid bundle behaves exactly as `d569c08`.
- **D-22 sha mismatch → refusal, never silent use:** corrupt vendored or downloaded bytes are never loaded and never auto-overwritten (tamper safety); the diagnostic's remedy (c) tells the user to delete the bad file and re-run, which re-enters the download path. Refusal on the DOWNLOADED bytes additionally means a hub/pin drift surfaces loudly rather than loading different weights than were measured.
- **D-23 Download target rule:** first EXISTING candidate dir is completed in place (a partial repo-relative or Drive copy heals itself); else fresh `MODELS_DIR/<engine>` — Drive-persistent when Drive is mounted, session-local otherwise (the same MODELS_DIR convention as QWEN_DRIVE_CACHE). Partial-bundle completion means e.g. 2-of-3 Drive copies download only the missing file.
- **D-24 qwen / openrouter_vision untouched:** `ensure_qwen` keeps its transformers-hub + QWEN_DRIVE_CACHE path (self-healing by design); the cloud client has no weights. `_ensure_engine_weights` is wired only into `ensure_kellenok`/`ensure_baberu`; a pin-less engine spec falls back to the byte-identical legacy `_resolve_vendored_engine_dir` (that path is now unreachable for the two vendored engines but kept as the registry's generic contract).
- **D-25 Incomplete-dir handling:** a candidate dir that exists but lacks files counts as "missing" for those files (download fills them) — the directive's binary (vendored / missing) is implemented at FILE granularity within the two documented locations.
- **D-26 Harness environment note:** the cell-5 full-exec harness ran on torch 2.9.1+cu128 (cpu device) from the sandbox wheel cache — the M6-B1 session's 2.14.1 env is gone; same real-torch full-cell-exec class as V3 of the M6-B1 record, and the torch-bearing spans of cell 5 are byte-identical to `d569c08` (proved by V1), so the prior real-torch record transfers.
- **D-27 Tests 26-29 battery count:** cell 7's battery grows 9/10 → 10/10 with Test 29 (the cell-8 battery stays exactly 25/25 and byte-untouched); Test 29 continues the D-11 placement convention (global numbering from cell 8, block in cell 7).
- **D-28 baberu ONNX trio not re-downloaded in-harness:** the harness re-fetched kellenok in full (3/3, sha == pins) + the baberu tokenizer pair (2/2) to prove the pinned revisions resolve and the shas still hold; the three baberu ONNX files (≈122 MB) were not re-fetched — E3 already live-verified that exact hub tree at the same pinned revision via the HF API, the vendored bytes sha-match the pins (V0), and the downloader code path is identical per-file. Cheap to extend if Flash wants the full re-fetch.
- **Known-opens (non-blocking):** auto-download needs `huggingface_hub` (Colab ships it via transformers; a fresh local env without it surfaces the import error inside the diagnostic — no cell break); `HF_TOKEN` is optional (public repos; the hub warns about rate limits, not a failure); the ~145 MB first-run download shares Colab bandwidth with qwen's hub fetches by design.

## PC1-C5 RF-8: onnxruntime in cell 0 + download hang robustness (Owner notebook-only live retest fix) (GLM-5.3, 2026-10-20)

Directive: PM relay — the Owner live-tested `mangabd-006-b1` @ `a0e6bbf` on a **fresh, notebook-only Colab session** (the Phase-D merge-gate item: exercise tier-2 auto-download end-to-end). Two real defects surfaced. [FACT]

**Defect 1 — onnxruntime missing from cell 0.** baberu's engine diagnostic correctly says "Install: `!pip install onnxruntime`" but cell 0's pip-install groups never included it, so on a fresh Colab the loader refuses gracefully but the first-run UX is a preventable engine-degradation. Fix: `"onnxruntime>=1.31.0"` added to `DETECTION_PACKAGES` in cell 0 (alongside pyclipper; the version GLM/Flash verified in RF-5/RF-7 = 1.31.0, floor-pinned per directive). Cell 0 was previously out of scope per the M6-B1 disclosure D-7 — this directive explicitly authorizes the cell-0 touch, superseding D-7. [FACT]

**Defect 2 — kellenok download hangs post-completion.** Owner log: `det/manga_det_v0.2.onnx: 100% 1.82M/1.82M [00:00<00:0` … then hung 10+ minutes: `hf_hub_download` completed but the subsequent processing (RF-7's cache-path → `shutil.copyfile` → target move, then sha-verify) blocked. Root-cause candidates from the directive: (a) the post-download copy blocking on a partially-locked cache dir; (b) sha re-read on a symlinked/hardlinked cache path with odd Colab access semantics; (c) a lock held by an interrupted previous attempt. **Fix (directive-literal, eliminates the class structurally):**
- `_download_engine_file` now calls `hf_hub_download(local_dir=<target>, force_download=False, revision=PINNED)` — HF stages inside `local_dir/.cache/huggingface/` and completes with a same-filesystem move into place; **there is no cache-path copy step at all anymore** (candidate (a) and (b) removed by construction; (c) is contained by the timeout below).
- **Per-file timeout** (`_ENGINE_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 300`): the call runs in a daemon worker thread + `join(timeout)`; on expiry a loud `RuntimeError` ("TIMEOUT: <file> download 300s-এ শেষ হয়নি … Kernel restart করে আবার চালাও — সম্পূর্ণ ফাইল sha-verify হয়ে থাকলে আর download হবে না, বাকিগুলো resume হবে।") propagates into the full RF-7 diagnostic — a hang can no longer be silent. Python threads are unkillable, so the timed-out worker lingers until it finishes or the kernel restarts (daemon ⇒ it never blocks interpreter exit); disclosed as D-31.
- **Per-file immediate sha-verify + visible progress**: after every downloaded file the loop verifies its sha against the pin and prints `[N/M] sha256=<8hex> size=<bytes> OK`; a mismatch raises at that file (fail-fast — the next file is never attempted) and the wrapped diagnostic's what-tried line now reports the stuck file + the completed count ("…শেষ চেষ্টা: <rel> (সম্পন্ন N/M)।").
- **Never-silent discipline**: ANY sha mismatch or timeout → the full RF-7 Bengali diagnostic (git-clone one-liner + Drive alternative); plus a post-download `dest.is_file()` guard that turns any local_dir/path-mismatch surprise into a loud error.
- The belt-and-braces full-bundle `_verify_engine_weights` after the loop is retained (pre-existing tampered files in a partial target are still caught there).

**Vendored-present behavior byte-unchanged (RF-7 guarantee inherited):** tier-1 (vendored complete → sha-verify → use, zero network) is untouched — `_resolve_vendored_engine_dir`, `ensure_kellenok`, `ensure_baberu`, the registry pins, and the `tried` string are byte-identical to `0b5d11d` (asserted as spans in V1), and the vendored R3 read reproduces the committed RF-5 measurement byte-exactly with identical stdout (V4).

**Test 29 extension (cell 7) — hermetic, no real network anywhere:**
- **Phase ঙ (new, the directive's hang simulation):** real `_download_engine_file` + monkey-patched `huggingface_hub.hf_hub_download` = `time.sleep(30)` stub + patched global `timeout = 1 s` → asserts the refusal carries the TIMEOUT marker + the patched value ("1s") + git-clone + Drive, and wall-clock < 10 s (the timeout genuinely fired rather than the sleep completing). hf_hub_download + the downloader + the timeout global are all restored in a `finally` (the daemon stub-thread dies silently ≤30 s later).
- **Phase ক (assertion update REQUIRED by fail-fast):** wrong-bytes downloads now stop at the FIRST file, so the downloader call count assertion changes 3→1 (kellenok + baberu both: exactly one call, at a pinned file, with pinned repo+rev).
- **Phase খ (progress assertion):** the fake-pin download flow runs under `contextlib.redirect_stdout` and asserts the `[1/2] sha256=…` / `[2/2] sha256=…` progress lines + ` OK`.
- Phases গ/ঘ (vendored-valid zero-network, vendored-corrupt refusal) unchanged.

**Verification — 81/81 ALL GREEN (`scripts/m006_b1_rf8_verify.py`):**
- **V0** 8/8 vendored-file sha256 (unchanged bytes).
- **V1 static:** AST 27/27; **24 non-target cells byte-identical to `0b5d11d`** (RF-8 touches exactly {0, 5, 7}); re-derivation equality (post == `0b5d11d` + exactly the 12 anchored patches, manifest `scripts/m006_b1_rf8_patches.json`); cell-0 pin present once in DETECTION_PACKAGES and absent everywhere else; timeout constant + downloader mechanics (local_dir/force_download/revision kwargs, daemon+join+is_alive, dest guard, patched-global read); per-file fail-fast verify + progress format; byte-untouched spans (`_resolve_vendored_engine_dir`, `ensure_kellenok`/`ensure_baberu`, `tried`, registry pins); Test-29 extension markers + hermeticity + restore discipline.
- **V2** non-source + top-level notebook fields identical.
- **V3 runtime harness** (cells 5 → 7 → 8, the RF-7 mocked-globals harness + the same disclosed single-substring `/root` cache redirect; torch 2.14.1+cpu — D-26 class): cell 5 battery **9/9**; cell 7 battery **10/10** (zero ❌; the hang-phase's 0-MB fake-pin download banner proves tier-2 ran inside the battery); cell 8 **25/25**; post-cell-7 restorations asserted (timeout constant 300, `hf_hub_download` original, real bound downloader, registry pins == manifest).
- **V4 vendored-present behavior:** kellenok manual R3 read **BYTE-EXACT vs the committed RF-5 measurement**, stdout identical to M6-B1/RF-7 (load banner, no download banner, no progress lines); baberu int4 manual R3 read.
- **V5 REAL-network self-heal behavior:** vendored-present downloader tripwire (0 calls); REAL `local_dir` downloads — kellenok **3/3** and **baberu FULL 5/5 (incl. the ONNX trio, ~122 MB — Phase-D observation D-28 discharged)**, every sha == pin, progress lines `[1/3]`..`[3/3]` and `[1/5]`..`[5/5]` with sha8+size+OK captured from the flow; offline re-run of the completed bundle returns the same dir with ZERO download output; the REAL timeout mechanism proven twice (direct call → TIMEOUT < 10 s; full `_ensure_engine_weights` → the complete Bengali diagnostic with git-clone + Drive + "সম্পন্ন 0/3"); wrong-bytes download → fail-fast refusal with the downloader called exactly ONCE; corrupt vendored → refusal, downloader never called.
- **V6** m002_sim_v2 on the post-build extraction: **ABORT-CLASS findings (unguarded, unresolvable): 0**.

**Interpretation disclosures for the Flash delta check:**
- **D-29 Cell-0 scope expansion:** cell 0 enters the touched set per this directive (D-7's "onnxruntime NOT in cell 0 (out of scope)" disclosure is superseded — the defect it documented is now fixed); the pin is the floor form `>=1.31.0` per the directive's literal text; placement in `DETECTION_PACKAGES` (the group carrying pyclipper, the OCR-det dependency group).
- **D-30 Fail-fast per-file verify (production-semantics delta, required by the directive's per-file progress + never-silent rules):** previously ALL missing files downloaded then verified together (a bad download still fetched the rest; Test 29 phase ক saw 3 downloader calls); now verification is immediate per file so a bad first file stops the loop (1 call). Refusal class, diagnostic content, and refusal triggers are unchanged — only the point in time the refusal fires moves earlier. Test 29 phase ক's call-count assertion updated 3→1 as a direct consequence (disclosed as a test-semantics change, not a coverage reduction: the pinned repo+rev+file assertion is retained).
- **D-31 Timeout mechanism:** daemon worker thread + `join(timeout)`; on expiry the main flow raises immediately but the worker is unkillable and may linger in the background (holds no shared state; dies at kernel restart; the diagnostic advises the restart). The timeout value is read at call time from the module global `_ENGINE_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 300) so the self-test can patch it to 1 s — the value is a floor, not a max: fast networks never see it; it exists to convert a silent 10-minute-plus hang into a loud failure.
- **D-32 local_dir staging side-effect:** HF writes its transfer metadata under `<target>/.cache/huggingface/` inside the models dir; tier-1 completeness checks and `_verify_engine_weights` look only at the pinned relpaths, so the staging dir is invisible to the contract (disclosed; harmless on Colab/Drive).
- **D-33 Progress-line semantics:** M = files missing in THIS run (a partial bundle shows the remaining count; the banner keeps the missing/pins ratio); N counts completed downloads; the printed sha is the actual on-disk sha (== the pin, else refusal fires first) and the size is the byte count.
- **D-34 baberu ONNX trio re-fetched in-harness (D-28 discharged):** the RF-8 verify re-downloaded the FULL baberu int4 bundle (5/5, ~122 MB, every sha == pin) through the NEW local_dir path — the RF-7 gap (only the tokenizer pair was re-fetched) is closed; kellenok full 3/3 re-verified through the new path as well.
- **D-35 Harness environment:** torch 2.14.1+cpu (the RF-7 session's 2.9.1+cu128 env was recycled with the sandbox; same real-torch full-cell-exec class, D-26 lineage), onnxruntime 1.31.0 (the pin), huggingface_hub 2.2.0, transformers 5.20.2; the torch-bearing spans of cell 5 are byte-identical to `0b5d11d` (V1), so the prior real-torch records transfer.
- **Known-opens (non-blocking, carried):** the Phase-D cross-candidate mixed-state redundant-download nit (a candidate dir with SOME valid files while another candidate holds the complete bundle → tier-2 tops up instead of switching) — out of RF-8 scope, unchanged; HF rate-limit warning on unauthenticated downloads (harmless, `HF_TOKEN` optional); the ~145 MB first-run download shares Colab bandwidth with qwen's hub fetches by design.

## PC2-0 What was measured before any code was written

The directive's ALGORITHM SPEC was implemented verbatim first (center = x + width/2; centers sorted DESC; band boundary gap > median-gap × 1.5; single-column pages one band; y-ASC within band) and run against the archived detections geometry (`scripts/m006_b2_algo_check.py`): on S003 it computes **[3, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6]** — the vertical T/L note R1 (center 40.5) has a center-gap of 87 to the left column, equal to the median gap, so it is NOT split into its own band; Kendall tau vs the VLM-confirmed order **+0.467**. The directive's own VERIFICATION section requires the computed order to match R3→R2→R4→R5→R6→R1 (the Phase A reference tau +1.000) — the literal spec is self-contradictory on the archived corpus. The Phase A §M4.1 form the directive cites as its source ("ALGORITHM SPEC (from Phase A §T2)") — x-interval-overlap connected-component bands → bands right-to-left (max right edge DESC) → top-to-bottom within band (y ASC, x-DESC tie) → overlay margin notes configurable (D2 default geometric) — reproduces the VLM-confirmed order EXACTLY. Per project protocol the binding gate wins; the deviation is disclosed here with the measured evidence. [TEST RESULT]
