## PC2-2 Scope deviations + out-of-scope sites (disclosed with justification)

1. **Cell 6: zero changes** (the directive estimated "cell 6: build_ocr_row emits reading_order" — `build_ocr_row` verifiably lives in **cell 7**, where the directive itself assigns the compute; the real cell-6 content is `detect_page`, which writes detections.json and runs the pre-existing LTR detection sort that assigns ids). No change is needed there: the reading_order index is purely additive on top of the existing ids; detection id assignment and every archived sample's id mapping stay byte-stable. The directive's own cell-7 line ("ocr_page computes and assigns … uses detections.json geometry") is exactly what was implemented.
2. **Cell 17: zero changes** (outside the directive's set — analyzed, disclosed). `export_ai_text_safe` delegates to cell 8's `export_ai_text` in every normal configuration (cell_17:260–262), so the exported ai.Text inherits reading order; its own `sort_values("id")` (cell_17:292) executes only in the degraded fallback when cell 8's export is absent. Touching it would violate the "Do NOT touch cells outside this set" rule for zero normal-path gain; flagged for a future batch if the fallback path ever becomes reachable.
3. **Cell 10 (render) + cell 19 (file apply): zero changes.** Render draws by geometry (position-keyed, order-independent); apply from file matches by [page:id] tag. The directive's "apply semantics unchanged" hard requirement is satisfied by NOT touching them.
4. **Algorithm spec deviation** — §PC2-0 above: directive's literal center-gap form fails its own S003 gate (tau +0.467); the Phase A §M4.1 validated form implemented (EXACT on all archived samples). Measured evidence: `scripts/m006_b2_algo_check.py` + `m006_bench/results/reading_order.json` (Phase A).

## PC2-3 Backward-compat proof (the directive's four hard requirements)

1. **Archived samples (S001..S008) load unchanged; missing `reading_order` computed lazily from geometry on first access.** The saved CSVs lack the column → `load_translation_df`'s generic schema loop fills `""` → `ensure_reading_order_column` normalizes to -1 and computes 0..N-1 per page from the rows' own x/y/width/height (cell_03:903–906 + 1140). The belt-and-braces layer: `_reading_order_series` inside `payload_sort_page_rows` recomputes ANY slice whose values are missing/blank/-1 even if the df came from a path that skipped the ensure hooks — proven by Test 30 (a df built WITHOUT the column sorts [3,2,4,5,6,1]). Archived detections.json/ocr.json without the field load unchanged (additive reads); they gain the field only when a page is re-OCR'd with force.
2. **Old ai.Text files parse unchanged.** `parse_ai_text` / `_parse_ai_text_local` match by `[page:id]` tag; no order or count assumption exists in the parser (cell 8/cell 22, untouched bytes).
3. **Apply semantics unchanged.** `apply_manual_translation` keys on `(page_id, id)`; `apply_translations_from_text` parses to the same keys — never list position. Only the DISPLAY of the coverage report is ordered (cell_22:492).
4. **V-3 vertical note handling preserved.** The V-3 predicate (cell_07:404, MANGABD-003 B2) and its rotation-ladder line order are untouched; vertical regions are placed by their own geometry (S003's R1 = own leftmost margin band → last, the VLM-confirmed position) and their in-region line sequence stays exactly what the V-3 ladder decoded.

## PC7B-1 The changes (6 anchored patches, cells {5, 8} ONLY)

Build driver `scripts/m007_b2_build.py` + content modules `scripts/m007_b2_content5.py` / `scripts/m007_b2_content8.py`; every anchor unique-asserted in its cell; the full (cell, name, old, new) manifest lands in `scripts/m007_b2_patches.json` (6 patches: 2 in cell 5, 4 in cell 8) and the verifier re-derives `new == old @ dcd96c0 + exactly these patches`; JSON writer proven byte-faithful on the pre-edit file; 25 non-target cells byte-identical; AST 27/27 warnings-as-errors; cell-5 2028 → 2240 lines (+212), cell-8 2961 → 3092 (+131). - **S05-1 (cell 5) — `ModelManager.ensure_embedding()` CREATED ("create if not exists"; it did not exist — verified zero embedding/semantic-search code anywhere in the notebook pre-change) + `_embedding_load_failed(exc)` helper**, inserted between `ensure_openrouter_vision` and the `unload_*` group (the ensure_* family convention). Behavior per the directive: lazy `from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer` inside try; `ImportError` and `OSError` caught explicitly, plus broad `Exception` with the identical message (disclosed §PC7B-2c); every failure path routes to `_embedding_load_failed`, which records `self.errors["embedding"]`, drops any partial instance, and emits the directive's EXACT Bengali diagnostic template — "⚠️ Embedding model load failed: <reason>। Feature disabled: semantic search। Fix: !pip install sentence-transformers OR check internet connection।" — BOTH printed to console AND logged at level=ERROR, then **returns `None`; never raises** (graceful degrade). Additional design within spec: (a) **cached-failure short-circuit** — a failed load is remembered in `self.errors["embedding"]`, so repeated calls return `None` immediately without re-attempting a doomed import/download (proven: repeat call adds zero log lines); `force=True` clears the error and retries (and a later success pops the cached error); (b) the model name resolves via safe `.get()` chains — `MANGABD_CONFIG["semantic_search"]["embedding_model"]`, default `sentence-transformers/paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2` (multilingual incl. Bengali, small) — so **NO cell-1 config change is needed** (scope discipline: cells {5, 8} only); cache_dir = `MODELS_DIR / "embedding"`, consistent with the cell's dir-constant convention. Caller contract (docstring): `emb = model_manager.ensure_embedding()`; `emb is None` ⇒ the semantic-search feature returns an empty result, never a crash. [FACT]
- **S05-1 (cell 5) — NEW Test 10 "Embedding graceful degrade (S05-1)"** appended to the §7 self-test battery (9 existing + 1 new = 10; `run_test` ledger). Deterministic, environment-independent, disk/network/install-state inert: (a) **ImportError simulated** via the standard `sys.modules["sentence_transformers"] = None` injection (CPython raises "import … halted; None in sys.modules" — the exact "package missing" signal), (b) **OSError simulated** via a fake `sentence_transformers` module whose `SentenceTransformer` raises `OSError` on construction (the network/download-failure class); each sim asserts `ensure_embedding()` returns `None`, `"embedding" not in instances`, `errors["embedding"]` populated, and the exact Bengali diagnostic present in BOTH the captured log and the captured console; module state restored in inner `finally`s; the harness global `log_event` is swapped for a capture and restored in the outer `finally` (the RF-2-proven save/restore discipline); plus the cached-failure short-circuit (repeat call → `None`, zero new log lines) and post-failure manager usability (`status()` still returns its dict). [TEST RESULT]
- **S05-2 (cell 8) — `parse_ai_text` B2-step-1 unescape pipeline hardened** per the directive's numbered spec: **(1) iterative unescape-to-stability** — the single `.replace` pass became a `while "\\[" in line or "\\]" in line` loop (each pass strictly shortens the line while any `\[/\]` pair remains, so termination is provable; a full pass that changes nothing breaks as "stable"), so **double-escaped** `\\[01.jpg:1\\]` now converges to `[01.jpg:1]` (single-pass left exactly one escape level behind — the disclosed gap); `stats["unescaped"]` keeps its per-line counting semantics (Tests 16/17 byte-parity); **(2) residual-escape validation, WARN-never-fail** — after convergence, a residual `\[`/`\]` increments the new visible counter `escape_warn`, emits a `log_event(..., level="WARN")` + a visible console line, and the line continues to the parse (where a non-matching tag is still visibly reported per V-4a — never silent); **(3) trailing line-end backslash strip before the tag regex** — `line.rstrip("\\")` (whole run) with the new visible counter `trailing_backslash`, so `text → বাংলা\` no longer poisons the tag match or the translation tail; ordering per the directive's numbering (unescape → validate → strip → parse) — note a lone trailing `\[` is UNESCAPED (not stripped), preserving bracket content to visible unparsed reporting; **(4) Tests 31-32** — Test 31 double-escaped fixture (2 lines, `\\\\[…\\\\]` in source = `\\[…\\]` content) → 2/2 entries, zero unparsed; Test 32 mixed-escape fixture (escaped line + raw line + trailing-backslash line + escaped arrowless Bengali-only line — the directive's three edge shapes in one paste) → 4/4 entries, `unescaped=2, arrowless=1, trailing_backslash=1, escape_warn=0`, zero unparsed. Battery: 30 existing + 2 new = **32/32**; sandbox pattern = Tests 16-21 (deterministic empty-session `translation_df`, save/restore in finally). Docstring step-1 note + both new counters ride the `("_unparsed", 0)` sentinel report (V-4a never-silent). [TEST RESULT]

[FACT]

## §M8-A1 — CODE-PATH MAP (directive item 1: locate the exact render/typesetting path)

The render path is **not** cells 10/11 alone — it is a chain with a guarded monkey-patch, exactly as the MANGABD-001 audit foretold ("render_bengali_text monkey-patched (guarded, persists)"):

| Path | Cell / lines (0-based code-cell index, current bytes) | Font logic | Fallback? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Base renderer (horizontal)** | cell 10 §8: `render_bengali_text` **L552–669**; drawn via `PIL ImageDraw.Draw.text` (L632–648, stroke variants L650–667) | `get_font` **L179–214** → `MANGABD_CONFIG["rendering"]["font_regular"|"font_bold"]` (NotoSansBengali Regular/Bold) via `ImageFont.truetype`, `_FONT_CACHE` L176 | **NO — single Bengali font for the entire string** |
| **Rotated path** | cell 10: `_render_rotated_text` **L672–779** (angle \|·\|>1.0°), RGBA canvas → `rotate(angle, BICUBIC)` → paste (L743–755); exception fallback re-calls the base renderer at angle 0 (L766) | same `get_font` object passed in | **NO** |
| **Vertical path (V-3, MANGABD-003 B2/V-1)** | cell 11 "QUALITY CORE FINAL" §7: `_main_font_ok` **L134–137**, `_split_runs` L138–147, `get_font_fb` **L154–167**, `_qc_render_vertical` **L168–207**; guarded patch `render_bengali_text` **L210–221** (`_QC_FINAL_RENDER_PATCHED`); trigger **L215**: `w < 90 and h > 2*max(1, w) and abs(angle) <= 1.0` | **per-run dual-font**: main runs → `get_font` (Bengali); non-main runs → `get_font_fb` (NotoSansJP variable font, `set_variation_by_axes([wght])`, wght default 600 → 700+ if bold; V-1 knobs `fallback_cjk_scale` 1.25 + `fallback_cjk_stroke` 1, L150–152); column canvas `(h, w)` → per-run measure (L178–193, the Flash amendment-1 overflow ladder) → baseline align `ly + asc_main − asc_fb` (L199, amendment-2) → `rotate(-90, BICUBIC)` → paste (L203–206) | **YES — the only path with per-glyph (per-run) fallback** |
| **Vertical mirror (older iteration)** | cell 12 "QUALITY CORE" L88–178 — byte-equivalent logic, guard `_QC_RENDER_PATCHED`; both patches chain at runtime (cell-12's `_qc_orig_render` L168 captures cell-11's wrapper as its base) — net behavior identical, the vertical predicate is the same L172 | same | same |
| **Orchestrators (no font logic)** | cell 13 (RESIDUAL KILLER + `run_inpaint_render_all` redef), cell 15 (UI/pipeline, cv2.putText debug overlays only), cell 23 (translate-stage-sync redef) — the `run_inpaint_render_all` chain redefined 5× (cells 11, 12, 13, 15, 23) per MANGABD-001; none touch fonts | n/a | n/a |

**Answer to the directive's explicit question: the V-3 vertical path and the horizontal/rotated paths do NOT share font logic.** The vertical path routes per-run between two fonts; the horizontal and rotated paths draw the whole string with the single Bengali font. That asymmetry IS the defect surface. Draw mechanism: PIL `ImageDraw.text` under libraqm (verified `PIL.features.check("raqm")` = True here; cell-0 L444–445 makes raqm a hard startup gate) — **not** freetype-py, **not** cv2.putText (cv2 `FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX` appears only in debug/QC overlays in cells 6/7/15/17 — Latin-only, never draws translated text). Entry gate: cell 10 `render_page` **L874–883** — `render_validate_bengali` skips any region whose text contains zero Bengali chars, so kana-only regions never render at all; mixed Bengali+kana passes. [FACT]

## §M8-A2 — FONT STACK + ASSET INVENTORY (directive items 2 & 4)

- **Registered assets** — `MANGABD_ASSETS["fonts"]` (cell 4 **L493–506**): `NotoSansBengali-Regular.ttf` + `NotoSansBengali-Bold.ttf` ONLY (paths + regular_ok/bold_ok/raqm_ok/render_ok flags; downloaded cell 4 L398–429, 3 URLs each, bold falls back to a copy of regular L423–427). **The Japanese-capable font is NOT registered** — `NotoSansJP.ttf` is downloaded into the same `DIRS["fonts"]` dir (BASE_DIR/fonts, cell 1 L117–121) by cells 11/12 **L17–26** (google/fonts `NotoSansJP[wght].ttf`, the variable font) and only recorded in `MANGABD_CONFIG["rendering"]["font_fallback"]` — an inventory gap. `MANGABD_CONFIG["rendering"]["font_fallback"]` is set **unconditionally, even when the download fails** (L20–26: warning print only, then assignment).
- **Repo state:** ZERO fonts vendored (`git ls-files` has no ttf/otf) — all fonts are runtime downloads; the repo already vendors ONNX weights under `models/` (M6-B1 precedent), so vendoring a font is precedent-compatible.
- **So: is a Japanese-capable font already present?** YES at runtime-if-download-succeeds (NotoSansJP, since MANGABD-003 B1/V-1) — that is exactly why kana in vertical notes render; NO font with kana is registered/vendored, and the **fallback download failure silently degrades to Bengali-only** (§M8-A3 D-4).
- **Glyph coverage measured (fontTools cmap, deterministic; `scripts/m008_b1_kana_repro.py` probe):** NotoSansBengali-Regular **contains NO basic Latin and NO dash/arrow block** — of the archived texts' chars, **26 distinct chars are absent**: `"` `(` `)` `,` `/` `:` + letters A E H O R T + `—` U+2014 + the 14 kana あ い お が こ し た て ね み ゅ ら る (kept from Latin: **only `!` `?`**); it DOES contain `।` U+0964 and `॥` U+0965 (Devanagari block — shared punctuation). NotoSansJP contains **all 26** (danda/ double-danda are its gaps). **Chars absent from BOTH fonts, across all 36 archived texts (77 distinct chars): ZERO** — except `➔` U+2794 (not in any archived text) which is absent from both. Every `_main_font_ok` explicit-list char `→⇒➔➡—–…` (cells 11/12 L137) is **absent from NotoSansBengali** — the list routes them to a font that cannot draw them. [TEST RESULT]

## §M8-A3 — ROOT CAUSE, PROVEN (directive item 3) — NOT HarfBuzz; a three-layer coverage/routing defect

**NOT (c) shaping/HarfBuzz.** libraqm is present and hard-gated (cell 0 L444–445); Bengali conjunct shaping is correct in every archived final (Owner-accepted through S002–S007); the tofu boxes are `.notdef` glyph absences (hollow rectangles), not mis-shapings; danda renders perfectly wherever the Bengali font draws it (e.g. horizontal R6, Flash S005 record). The root cause is **(a)+(b) combined, split by path**:

- **D-1 (the N-D of record) — danda misroute in the vertical path.** `_main_font_ok` (cells 11/12 **L134–137**) classifies main-font chars as Bengali block `0x0980–0x09FF` + Latin `0x0000–0x00FF` + punct `0x2000–0x206F` + currency + the explicit arrow/dash list. `।` = **U+0964, Devanagari block — OUTSIDE 0x0980–0x09FF** → classified non-main → drawn with NotoSansJP → **JP has no U+0964/U+0965** (cmap-proven) → hollow tofu box. Flash's MANGABD-004 record already named this ("danda after a kana run resolves to a CJK-font run without U+0964", GLM_5_3_FLASH L1569) — the mechanism is per-char classification, so it fires for **every danda in a vertical note**, not only after kana runs. [FACT + TEST RESULT]
- **D-2 (NEW, proven this phase) — ASCII/dash misroute in the vertical path.** NotoSansBengali has no basic Latin: every `"` `,` `/` `:` `(` `)` letter/digit — routed MAIN by the `0x0000–0x00FF` clause — draws as a Bengali-font `.notdef` tofu box; `—` U+2014 (in the archived texts) likewise. Visible in the archived finals (see §M8-A4 X-probes + the S007 R1 box fragments at the `/` `:` positions and the **S007 R2 horizontal-bubble `"` tofu box found at page (271, 232)**). This was never enumerated in the N-D backlog records (the "kana dark blocks" signature absorbed it). [FACT + TEST RESULT]
- **D-3 (latent for kana, LIVE for ASCII) — no fallback on the horizontal/rotated paths.** Cell 10's base renderer has no run-splitting: any char missing from NotoSansBengali tofus. For **kana** this is latent (no archived horizontal region contains kana yet — all archived kana live in the R1 vertical notes); for **ASCII/dash** it is live since Batch 1 (the archived R2 `"` box). Reproduced deterministically on current bytes: S3 render of the S007 R1 text through the unpatched base path → kana `こ` matches the NotoSansBengali `.notdef` template at **NCC 1.000** and danda matches the Bengali danda at **1.000** (control). [TEST RESULT]
- **D-4 (silent-degrade hazard) — `get_font_fb`'s except-arm.** On `ImageFont.truetype(font_fallback)` failure it silently returns `get_font(size, bold)` (the Bengali font) — combined with the unconditional config assignment (§M8-A2), a runtime download failure converts the whole vertical path to kana-tofu with **zero render-time warning** (one console print at cell-exec time only). Reproduced: S2 (fallback path pointed at a missing file) — kana runs match the Bengali `.notdef` references, JP references score ~0. [TEST RESULT]
- **D-5 (edge, new) — baseline-align clip.** The V-1 amendment-2 anchor `ly + asc_main − asc_fb` (cells 11/12 **L199**) goes **negative** when the scaled fallback's ascent exceeds `ly + asc_main` — proven: the S4a short-text control fits at size 33 → danda run `tc_y = −13` → the tofu box is partially clipped above the canvas top edge. Latent (all archived notes fit at ~8–11 px where tc_y stays positive); deterministic arithmetic from the verbatim cell-11 source. [TEST RESULT]

**And the "kana as dark blocks, seated inline" Batch-1 signature (the directive's visible symptom) is NOT tofu since V-1:** the archived S002–S007 kana are REAL glyphs rendered by NotoSansJP at wght 600 / scale 1.25 / stroke 1 — dark by deliberate design (V-1's S001-era weight compensation, MANGABD-003 records; Flash's own V-1 measurement: "dark こがねい and おてあらい glyph runs", GLM_5_3_FLASH L1260). Pre-V-1 S001 was the true kana-tofu baseline. The actually-documented N-D tofu (MANGABD-004 S005 record) is the **trailing danda**, plus the ASCII boxes now enumerated (D-2). **Scope note for the ruling:** the Owner's directive framing ("kana become tofu because the Bengali render font lacks kana glyphs") matches the pre-V-1 state and the horizontal-path state (D-3); on current bytes the vertical-path kana render correctly and the residual tofu is danda + ASCII. Any fix must cover all of D-1..D-5, not kana alone. [FACT]

## §M8-A4 — MINIMAL REPRODUCTION INSTRUMENT + RESULTS (directive item 3 proof; committed to `scripts/m008_b1_kana_repro.py`)

Instrument design: byte-faithful extraction of cell-10 §3–§8 + cell-11 §7 source spans from the CURRENT notebook (md5-gated), exec'd verbatim in an instrument namespace (minimal `MANGABD_CONFIG` + `log_event` stub; `vertical_rotate: -90` re-seeded to mirror cell-11 §2 L27) — the reproduction runs the REAL current code path, not a re-implementation. - **Probes (deterministic):** cmap coverage table (§M8-A2) + `_main_font_ok` routing table — `।`/`॥`/kana → FALLBACK, `ক`/`a`/`—`/`→` → MAIN (the two misroutes in one line) + `render_validate_bengali`: kana-only → False (skipped), mixed → True. [TEST RESULT]
- **S1 — vertical, fallback present, S007 R1 text (the N-D live case):** fitted size 10; kana runs render via JP (real dark ink); the trailing danda run reproduces the N-D tofu — **hollow box confirmed at the computed position (crop x 29–36, y 169–183)** side-by-side with the `,` tofu (D-2); ASCII `:` `/` boxes at the line-0 start (D-2). S1b — S003 text (pure kana runs こがねい / おてあらい, no danda): all-kana runs render as real ink, no danda present → no danda tofu (matches the archived S003 final, which contains none). [TEST RESULT]
- **S2 — vertical, fallback file missing:** `get_font_fb` silently degrades to Bengali; kana runs now match the Bengali `.notdef` references (D-4 proven; zero render-time warnings raised). [TEST RESULT]
- **S3 — horizontal base path (cell-10 only), S007 text:** `こ` → Bengali `.notdef` NCC **1.000**; `।` → Bengali danda NCC **1.000** (JP refs 0.606/0.397 — rejected). The directive's literal claim, reproduced on current bytes (D-3). [TEST RESULT]
- **S4 — danda-only control:** vertical → the danda run draws at `tc_y = −13` (D-5 clip marker, CLIP-RISK flagged by the instrument) with a partial box; horizontal → Bengali danda NCC **1.000** (danda fine wherever the Bengali font draws it). [TEST RESULT]
- **X — archived-crop cross-check (multi-scale grayscale NCC, stroke variants):** the NotoSansJP `.notdef` danda box template matches inside the R1 crops of **all three** danda-bearing archived finals — S005_manual **0.875**, S005_openrouter **0.771**, S007 **0.880** (probe scale ~8 px = the archived fitted sizes; the S003 final correctly shows no danda match — its R1 text has no danda). Corroborates: the archived N-D tofu IS the JP-font `.notdef` for `।`. [TEST RESULT]

**Disclosures:** (i) the per-run NCC classifier is noisy at the archived tiny fitted sizes (10–12 px kana/danda boxes vs ~6 px dense glyph fragments — scores 0.2–0.7, verdicts NOT relied upon); the decisive evidence is the deterministic probes, the S3/S4-horizontal NCC-1.000 matches, the X archived cross-checks, and the pixel-level zoomes at computed positions (S1 danda box; S007 R2 `"` box). (ii) The sandbox Bengali font is the jsdelivr mirror of the same notofonts hinted release (GitHub raw blocked here); cmap facts are release-stable; the archived-final box fragments at the `/` `:` `"` positions independently confirm the runtime font also lacks Latin. (iii) PIL 11.3.0 + raqm here vs the Colab runtime — `.notdef` rendering does not depend on raqm. (iv) Instrument outputs (PNG crops + report.json) are workspace artifacts, not committed. [FACT]

## §M8-A5 — FIX APPROACHES (directive items 5–7)

| | (a) Per-glyph composite fallback — extend V-1 to all paths + coverage-based routing | (b) Single merged font (Bengali+JP subset merge) | (c) Pre-transliterate kana → Bengali before render |
|---|---|---|---|
| Asset | NotoSansJP[wght].ttf — **already in-repo behavior** (cells 11/12 download); optionally vendor (9,589,900 bytes) | New build artifact (fonttools subset+merge), ~6–10 MB, must vendor + provenance chain | None |
| License | **OFL-1.1** (permissive; no Open-RAIL-M anywhere) — same family as the existing Bengali fonts | OFL-1.1 both sides, merge is license-clean but the merged file needs its own provenance | n/a |
| VRAM / startup | Zero VRAM; ~10–20 MB RAM; <50 ms cached load (CPU-only asset) | Same class | Same |
| Complexity | Moderate: (1) replace `_main_font_ok` ranges with **cmap-coverage routing** (main font has glyph → main; else fallback; neither → WARN); (2) gate the run-splitting into the horizontal/rotated paths only when a line contains non-main chars (pure-Bengali lines keep the exact current code path — zero behavior change for the 99% case); (3) `get_font_fb` WARN-never-silent on load failure; (4) clamp `ly_run ≥ 0` (D-5); new self-tests | High: new font build/maintenance, re-acceptance of the ENTIRE render surface (wrap/fit/stroke/darkness), loses the variable wght axis (freeze instances), loses the Owner-accepted V-1 weight knobs | Trivial but wrong |
| Risk to hard constraints | **None**: Bengali runs unchanged (conjuncts, danda — now routed main — and M004-B1 size/weight calibration all preserved; main-font metrics byte-identical); V-3 vertical arithmetic unchanged (only the routing table + the clamp); reading order untouched (different cells); V-1 knobs preserved | **High**: any metric change re-opens M004-B1 size/weight + wrap calibration; danda/conjunct regression risk re-introduced by the merge | High: destroys T/L-note content; does nothing for ASCII/dash (the larger live surface) |
| Fix coverage | **All of D-1..D-5** (vertical-path kana already worked) + horizontal kana + ASCII + danda, both paths | D-1..D-3 (D-4 becomes moot-ish: single file; D-5 moot) but with re-acceptance cost | None of the real surface |

**(c) is disqualified by the directive's own note — it defeats a T/L note whose purpose IS to show kana spelling, and it leaves the ASCII/dash surface untouched.** **(b) is dominated by (a)**: it buys nothing (a) doesn't, while re-opening every hard constraint the directive freezes. **RECOMMENDATION: (a)** — with the 4 changes above; Phase C scope proposal: cells 11/12 (mirrored routing + clamp + WARN) + cell 10 (the gated run-split helper for horizontal/rotated) + cell 4 (register the JP font in `MANGABD_ASSETS`) + new embedded self-tests (danda-vertical, ASCII-horizontal, coverage-routing table, fallback-load WARN, clip clamp); optional vendoring of NotoSansJP (9.6 MB ≪ GitHub 100 MB limit, M6-B1 weight-vendoring precedent) to structurally kill D-4 — Owner decision.

## §M8-A6 — DISPOSITION

**MANGABD-008 B1 PHASE A DELIVERED — docs + instrument only, ZERO source changes** (staged: this record + `scripts/m008_b1_kana_repro.py`; notebook blob identity to `63259a8` asserted by the instrument at load). Root cause proven and larger than the backlog label: the N-D class is a **two-font glyph-coverage + routing defect across all three draw paths** (danda misroute + ASCII/dash misroute + no-fallback horizontal/rotated + silent-degrade + baseline-clip), NOT kana-tofu on current bytes (vertical kana have rendered correctly since V-1). Approach (a) recommended with justification; no new font asset required at runtime (optional vendoring flagged for the Owner). **STOP — awaiting PM/Owner ruling before any implementation (Phase B/C).** [FACT]

The stray messages are designed fixture behavior fired through the logging/stderr channel (log_event uses `logger.log`, not print — `redirect_stdout` cannot capture it, and Colab's output area can displace stderr lines): the ERROR is Test 10's own D-4 WARN-never-silent proof — its assertion REQUIRES `warned=True` — and the after-D-4 WARN is Test 11's D-3 canvas_b range-routing under its intentional missing-path pin. [FACT + TEST RESULT]

**Root cause:** Tests 9/12 (and cells-11/12 §7.5 D-2) hardcode the DEFAULT font set's charset SHAPE — "main must lack ASCII" and "main .notdef must be hollow" — while the Owner's Drive `font_regular` file is a Latin-containing Bengali font: the CORRECT routing it produces (ASCII → main, tofu-free) fails the shape assertions, and the final gate hard-blocks a healthy pipeline. The production-path gap: cell 4 §8 downloads only when font_regular is MISSING — any other/wrong file at that path passes silently through cells 1–9. This is the main-font mirror of the RF-11 fallback story (wrong bytes at the fallback path passed silently until §8.5 sha-pinned them). [FACT]

**The fix (10 anchored patches, scope {4,10,11,12}; manifest re-derivation-equal; §7.5 mirror parity asserted):** (1) cell-4 **§8.6 main-font sanity check** — PIL load + fontTools cmap; ক/। required; a non-Bengali file at font_regular → loud named ❌ + WARN event + the delete/re-download fix route; Latin coverage → the loud "basic Latin আছে" shape note (tofu-free); registry key `regular_sane`; never raises (§8.5 contract). (2) cell-10 **Test 9** — HARD = render integrity: the RF-12 DIRECT probe kept + a tofu-scan (each of ক । a " — こ must be in ≥1 cmap — tofu chars NAMED in the ❌ details); DIAG = the D-2 shape (ASCII/kana present in main). (3) cell-10 **Test 12** — HARD = fb solid pixel-probe on BOTH "H" AND "こ" (fontTools-independent garbage guard; probe throw keeps the RF-12 loud-FAIL contract); the main-side .notdef calibration becomes a 3-outcome DIAG (hollow → silent pass / blank notdef / solid substitute / covers-all), never a hard fail — a font property, not render integrity; tofu responsibility = T1 main load + T9 tofu-scan + this fb probe (a solid-box-notdef main is fully covered by T9's tofu-scan, so no no-false-pass gap opens). (4) **gate decoupling** — new `run_test_diag` (loud 🔶 + counted in `tests_diagnosed`, NEVER in the pass/fail ledger; skip≠silence philosophy extended), final-status 🔶 line, `log_event` extra; `all_tests_passed = all(tests)` + the raise are byte-unchanged — the ledger now contains hard render-integrity failures only, so a tofu-free pipeline proceeds whatever the font-set shape. (5) cell-10 **post-battery fixture hygiene** — pop the fixture-scoped `_FONT_COVERAGE_CACHE["/tmp/MISSING…"]` entry + `_FONT_ROUTE_WARNED["fb_load"]` (the only residues the trace found). (6) cells-11/12 **§7.5 D-2** (byte-identical mirrors) — re-scoped to tofu-semantics (`_m8_cov_has`: glyph in ≥1 font; degraded-unknown → optimistic, matching `_main_font_ok`'s philosophy) + the "basic Latin" INFO note. [FACT]

**Verification 35/35 ALL GREEN** (`scripts/m008_b1_rf13_verify.py`, delta base `5a84994`): V1 static 16 — scope {4,10,11,12} exact; cell-4 whole-cell delta = §8.6 block + the one registry key line (§8.5 span + §9 span byte-identical); cell-10 §1–§9 + Tests 1-8/10/11 blocks byte-identical; RF-12 clear/pin/probe patterns (clears=10, 2 DIRECT probes, clear→pin→probe in both bodies, fb hollow probes H+こ) + RF-11 skip machinery + RF-10 D-4 asserts intact; gate mechanics byte-unchanged; AST 27/27 warnings-as-errors; vendored sha/size untouched; `m002_sim_v2` = 0 abort sites. V2 runtime 9 — **R-G OWNER CLASS: 12/12 hard PASS + 🔶 DIAG≥2 + NO gate raise** (the directive-5 decouple, with the diag lines naming the Latin shape + the final-status line + `tests_diagnosed` extras); healthy env 12/12 zero-diag; main=JP bytes → T9 hard-fail NAMING U+0995/U+0964 + gate raise; stale-bad ambient → T10+T11 loud + gate raise; garbage-at-vendored → T9+T12 loud with exception details + gate raise (Flash's no-false-pass finding stays covered — now double-guarded by the こ pixel-probe); vendored-missing → 9/12 ⚪ SKIP + NO gate raise (RF-11 class); `_FB_CACHE` pre-pollution → still 12/12 (RF-12 class). V3 §8.6 harness 5/5 (healthy sanity-OK / Latin note+INFO / JP-at-regular glyph-missing+WARN / missing / unloadable). V4 §7.5 mirrors both healthy 5/5; Latin-main: D-2 ✅ + INFO note, the single ❌ exactly D-5. V5 mutation trace — 8 writes = the 4 pin/restore pairs, first MISSING at Test 10 (after T9), post-battery residue popped. [TEST RESULT]

**Disclosures:** (i) The Owner's actual font file identity is an env fact this sandbox cannot read — the R-G CLASS (Latin-containing main) is what the code handles; FreeSerif is the in-sandbox class representative, and its SOLID-box notdef exercises T12's substitute-branch DIAG (all three calibration outcomes handled). (ii) The §7.5 D-5 failure in the FreeSerif repro (drawn=37 vs ref=44 at fit=45/scale=1.25) is a REAL but SEPARATE production limitation — the vertical renderer's own design note "fit_font_size calibrates on main-font metrics only" means a non-box-notdef main over-sizes the fit and the kana glyph clips in the narrow column; §7.5 prints and does NOT gate; NOT observed in the Owner's env; deliberately OUT of RF-13 scope per the bounded-round rule — it becomes the next bounded RF if the Owner's font is in that subclass. (iii) The stderr-interleaving explanation for the ERROR's observed position is derived (trace: the message can only originate from Test 10's fixture call, which its own assertion requires to fire; log_event goes through logging→stderr) — the sandbox cannot observe Colab's display order directly. (iv) The ERROR line itself is REQUIRED by Test 10's D-4 contract and remains in the output post-fix (that is WARN-never-silent working); only its misreading as evidence of a fallback defect is corrected. (v) Test 9's D-1 semantics (danda in MAIN) is preserved through the tofu-scan for the default font set and becomes a coverage check (। in ≥1 font) + shape diag for non-default sets — danda-in-fallback-only renders tofu-free via the fallback, reported as shape. (vi) The RF-10 (22/22), RF-11 (30/30) and RF-12 (24/24) suites are superseded on the branch by this 35-check delta suite — all earlier instruments remain byte-identical as their committed records. [FACT + TEST RESULT]

**Disposition:** RF-13 complete — **Gate update (mid-flight):** while RF-13 was being verified, Flash's RF-12 DELTA CHECK landed on the branch (`c072dc5`): **RF-12 CLOSED** — 77 checks + 2 simulator runs ALL PASS (verbatim/segmented runtime matrix: happy 12/12; pre-pollution immunity across all 5 discovered keys; the post-T10 clear proven load-bearing; the RF-11 skip class preserved; garbage-at-vendored TIP fails loud with the exception named while BASE reproduces an observed 12/12 false-pass — the honest in-suite delta), scope {10} + the 3-patch manifest byte-re-derivation, m002_sim_v2 0/0; zero required fixes. Report-only (GLM_5_3_FLASH.md only, notebook blob == `5a84994`'s) — RF-13 re-parented onto it per the RF-11/RF-12-precedent docs-only-between topology; every `5a84994`-based byte proof in the RF-13 suite is unaffected. [FACT]

**Disposition:** RF-13 complete — ONE new commit on `mangabd-008-b1` with parent `c072dc5` (the Flash RF-12 DELTA CHECK report — docs-only, its notebook == `5a84994`'s; NO amend of any previous commit) carrying the notebook, this record, the evidence record (`scripts/m008_b1_rf13_evidence.md`), and the RF-13 instrument set (repro + dump + build + 10-patch manifest + verify + precommit) — pushed for the Flash DELTA check (delta scope: cell-4 §8.6 + the registry key, cell-10 run_test_diag helper + Test 9/Test 12 fixture bodies + hygiene tail + status/log_event lines, cells-11/12 §7.5 D-2 check only). Expected live on the Owner's Colab: cell 4 prints the §8.6 line (their Drive font_regular will be named with the "basic Latin আছে" note if it is the R-G class — the 4-hour mystery visible at startup); cell-10 battery **12/12 hard PASS, NO gate raise**, with 🔶 shape-diag lines explaining exactly which font differs from the default set; cells 11/12 §7.5 5/5 (or 4/5 with D-5-only if their font is the blank-notdef subclass — the disclosed follow-up). # MANGABD-009 — PHASE A: SMART BUBBLE + STYLE + COLOR (GLM-5.3, 2026-10-27)

## M9-A.0 Scope, authorization, and deliverables

Owner live-translated a full chapter on the merged B1 pipeline (post-`7950ab5`) and requested three enhancements (PM relay, zai-web IM): **R1** smart bubble fill (priority #1 — rendered text too small / too sparse in bubbles, worst in large bubbles with short text), **R2** original-orientation + style inheritance, **R3** original ink-color capture (future color manhwa/webtoon). Phase A = investigation + design + ruling-grade measurement ONLY: **NO notebook changes**; implementation batches B1/B2/B3 start ONLY after the PM/Owner ruling. All line refs below are against main @ `5bde06b` (notebook file md5 `451c7cc3c606448b1534e715fcf6f72efe0078251b2c18dddb4655cecf725a85`, recorded in the results JSON; cells extracted to workspace `cells_m009_a/` for the investigation). Deliverables committed this session: this record + the v2 instrument `scripts/m009_b1_fill_measure.py` + its results `scripts/m009_b1_fill_measure_results.json` + the superseded v1 pair (`scripts/m009_fill_ratio.py` / `scripts/m009_fill_ratio_results.json`, kept as evidence of the metric-unification step).

## M9-A.1 R1 — Smart bubble fill (Owner priority #1)

### M9-A.1.1 Symptom, quantified on the archives with the unified metric

The v2 instrument measures, per bubble region of the archived S003/S007 runs, ONE metric family against ONE denominator (the bubble interior mask flood-filled from the INPAINTED canvas): `ink_fill` = dark-ink pixels / bubble area; `extent_fill` = ink extent-box / bubble area; `block_fill`/`block_fill_usable` = layout block (max line width × total line-box height) / bubble area / usable area (mask eroded by the margin band). The artist (original.jpg), the archived current render (final.jpg + a byte-faithful `fit_font_size` re-sim), and the proposed B1 layout are all reported in the same units. Measured (full table in the results JSON):

| region | bbox | artist ink / extent | final archive ink / extent | current sim (sz/lines, blkU) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S003-R2 | 87×156 | 0.190 / 0.581 | 0.051 / 0.518 | 17/5, 0.609 |
| S003-R3 | 204×216 | 0.229 / 0.723 | 0.091 / 0.559 | 38/3, 0.718 |
| S003-R4 | 103×109 | 0.169 / 0.639 | 0.048 / 0.341 | 22/2, 0.473 |
| S003-R5 | 128×140 | 0.193 / 0.700 | 0.049 / 0.433 | 15/4, 0.522 |
| S003-R6 | 131×204 | 0.181 / 0.719 | 0.048 / 0.519 | 12/9, 0.583 |
| S007-R2 | 87×156 | 0.191 / 0.582 | 0.053 / 0.486 | 17/5, 0.575 |
| S007-R3 | 204×216 | 0.229 / 0.750 | 0.073 / 0.461 | 34/3, 0.645 |
| S007-R4 | 103×109 | 0.169 / 0.639 | 0.050 / 0.489 | 19/3, 0.615 |
| S007-R5 | 128×140 | 0.193 / 0.699 | 0.048 / 0.503 | 15/5, 0.595 |
| S007-R6 | 131×204 | 0.181 / 0.720 | 0.052 / 0.533 | 12/10, 0.618 |

[TEST RESULT] Two findings matter for the ruling. (1) **The rendered Bengali carries only 25–35% of the artist's ink density** (0.048–0.091 vs 0.169–0.229) and the block occupies 0.47–0.72 of the usable area vs the artist's 0.64–0.75 extent — the under-filled archived class is R4/R5/R6 (+ S007-R2/R5), i.e. the mid-size and long-text bubbles; **R3-class 204×216 bubbles with substantial text are NOT the under-filled class** (0.645/0.718 blkU, near-artist extent). (2) The synthetic worst case the Owner's complaint maps to — short text (`কী হয়েছে?`) in a large 380×320 ellipse — measures **current blkU 0.184** at the production cap 48, geometrically starved.

### M9-A.1.2 Root cause (cell 10 §6, measured)

`fit_font_size` (cell 10 L543–621) fits text into an **axis-aligned rectangle derived from the bbox alone**: `padding = max(4, 0.08×min(w,h))` (L570–575), `aw = w − 2·padding`, `ah = h − 2·padding` (L577–578), then a binary search for the max size whose wrapped block fits that rectangle (L590–611, wrap via `split_text_to_lines` L479–532). Three consequences, all measured: (a) **no fill objective** — any size that merely fits is accepted up to the cap; nothing pushes toward the artist's ~0.7–0.85 occupancy; (b) **the rectangle ignores the bubble's true shape** — in an ellipse the top/bottom rows are far narrower than `aw`, so 3-line blocks can exceed the real usable area (v1 measured current line-box/usable = 1.143 on S003-R3 — overflow the mask makes visible), while conversely a short single line is width-starved by the rectangle when the bubble's middle band is the wide part; (c) **v1's metrics could not see any of this** — v1 split numerators/denominators across line-box/usable vs ink-extent/bubble and computed bubble masks from the ORIGINAL canvas where glyph strokes punch holes in the flood-fill (undershot areas), so its "new path never activated" (new == current on every region — the v1 results JSON, kept as evidence). The unified v2 metric family + the inpainted-canvas mask source fix both defects; v2's synthetic current-fill curve (0.184/0.326/0.415/0.461 at caps 48/64/72/96) independently reproduces v1's (0.186/0.329/0.418/0.463) — cross-instrument validation of the algorithm core. [TEST RESULT]

### M9-A.1.3 Mask source: the INPAINTED canvas

The bubble mask must come from the **inpainted** artifact (`inpainted.png` / `load_image_artifact(page_id, "inpainted")`, cell 10 L1023–1027) — the exact image `render_page` already loads and converts to the rendering canvas (L1045–1046), so a production B1 computes the mask at **zero extra I/O**. Text-free interiors flood-fill to clean, solid masks; measured against the original-canvas mask the bubble areas gain **+4.0% to +18.9%** (S003-R3 39,210→46,588 px; S007-R3 39,210→46,636; R5 +12.0/+12.2%; R4 +9.5%; R2 +8.8/+8.7%; R6 +4.0/+3.9%). The probe session also verified the interior row-width profiles are stable and full-width on the inpainted source (vs the shredded, hole-punched profiles from the original). [TEST RESULT]

### M9-A.1.4 B1 algorithm design (mask-aware adaptive layout)

1. **Bubble mask** — flood-fill the white interior of the inpainted canvas from bbox probe points (center + thirds, the `_snap_white_box` probe philosophy), 9×9 close, restrict to bbox±8 px.
2. **Usable mask** — erode the bubble mask by the margin band: `margin = max(3, round(0.03 × min(w,h)))` px via distance transform (proposed knob `rendering.bubble_fill_margin_ratio`).
3. **Per-line widths from the mask** — the block is vertically centered in the usable vertical extent; line i's band = the rows it occupies; band width = MIN row-span width across the band (ellipse/narrow-top aware; floor 10 px preserved from L577). The fixed `aw` rectangle disappears; each line gets the width the bubble actually has at that height.
4. **Maximize size** — binary search the max size s.t. every wrapped line fits its band width AND the block fits the usable vertical extent (wrap⇄band re-stabilization ≤8 rounds; word-level + grapheme-safe long-word split preserved from `split_text_to_lines`).
5. **Fill target** — `rendering.bubble_fill_target = 0.85` (of usable area) is the maximization goal, **not a guarantee**: the instrument reports `cap_bound` per region/cap when even the cap cannot reach it.
6. **Regression guards** — (a) accepted size ≥ current size (else keep current); (b) regions whose current `block_fill_usable` ≥ the under-fill trigger (`rendering.bubble_fill_trigger`, proposed 0.60) keep the EXACT current layout — byte-equal decisions, the MANGABD-004 small-bubble calibration untouched; (c) `font_size_min` floor kept; (d) mask empty/degenerate/no-white-interior → fall back to the current path unchanged.

### M9-A.1.5 Instrument results — the ruling surface (v2, @ cap 48 = today's production cap)

- **Triggered regions improve** (current blkU → proposed blkU @48; sizes; ink_fill): S003-R4 **0.473→0.653** (22→26 px; ink 0.052→0.069); S003-R5 **0.522→0.951** (15→19; 0.064→0.103 — reaches the 0.85 target); S003-R6 **0.583→0.777** (12→14; 0.061→0.095); S007-R2 **0.575→0.717** (17→19; 0.071→0.084); S007-R5 **0.595→0.950** (15→19; 0.064→0.102 — reaches target). Extent fills move toward the artist's (e.g. R5 0.79 vs artist 0.70; R4 0.47 vs 0.64).
- **Kept-current by the trigger guard**: S003-R2 (0.609), S003-R3 (0.718; also the size guard fired on the proposed 37<38 — the mask's narrow bottom rows cost 1 px vs the bbox rectangle there), S007-R3 (0.645; proposed == current 34 — the mask cannot do better within cap 48), S007-R4 (0.615), S007-R6 (0.618). **Trigger-policy data point:** at a 0.55 trigger S007-R4 (→22 px, 0.836) and S007-R6 (→13 px, 0.743) would also improve — the 0.60 vs 0.55 choice is a visible-coverage decision for the ruling.
- **Cap policy data:** at cap 48 the triggered regions land 0.65–0.95; the 0.85 target is reached only by the R5 class. Higher caps help only where the mask still has room (S003-R2 →0.702 already at 48; the synthetic case below is geometrically capped regardless of cap).
- **Synthetic worst case** (short text, 380×320 ellipse): current **0.184** (= the Owner-complaint class; matches v1's 0.186) → proposed **0.184 @48 / 0.326 @64 / 0.415 @72 / 0.461 @76+** — `cap_bound` at every cap incl. 160: a single short line in a large ellipse geometrically caps the centered block at ~0.46. **Design implication recorded:** `bubble_fill_target = 0.85` must be a maximization goal with a visible cap-bound disclosure (and an optional min-lines/multi-line layout search if the ruling wants to chase it), not a hard guarantee.

### M9-A.1.6 What B1 would touch in production (preview only — NOT implemented)

Cell 10 only: a mask-aware fit path alongside `fit_font_size` (config-gated, default OFF), the bubble-mask helper computed from the already-loaded `inpainted_bgr` in `render_page` before the region loop (L1045 ff.), the three new `rendering.bubble_fill_*` knobs, and per-region `cap_bound`/`kept_current` disclosures in the render meta (`mark_stage_done` meta L1154–1163 extension). Cells 11/12 (§7.5 mirrors) would inherit only if the ruling extends the scope. No detection/inpaint/OCR surface changes in B1 — the mask comes from the canvas the renderer already owns.

## M9-A.2 R2 — Original-orientation & style inheritance

**Current state (measured on the archives + cells):** `detections.json` already carries per-region `quad` (4-pt polygon) and `angle` fields — but both are **reconstructed, not measured**: cell 6 L1190–1207 builds `quad` as the axis-aligned bbox rectangle and hardcodes `angle = 0.0` (all archived rows are 0.0 with axis-aligned quads). The orientation information EXISTS upstream and is thrown away: the CTD detector returns per-textline quads (`textline.pts`, cell 6 L578) which are collapsed to min/max bboxes at L585–588; the `textlines` list survives only for mask dilation (`adaptive_dilate_mask` L716/L813/L850). The **renderer already supports arbitrary angles**: `render_bengali_text` gates `abs(angle) > 1.0` → `_render_rotated_text` (L776–792, RGBA canvas + rotate + paste, per-run fallback included since Phase C L903–919), and `render_page` already threads `row["angle"]` through (L1065, L1125) — the vertical 90° path (V-3, cells 11/12) is a separate, intact surface.

**B2 design:** at detection time compute a per-region dominant angle from its textline `pts` (top-edge vector angle per quad; median across the region's textlines; outliers >2σ rejected), store as `angle_deg` on the detection/ocr rows (data-model addition, schema-versioned), and let the existing angle plumbing render through `_render_rotated_text`. Style/emphasis inheritance: the CTD/kellenok class + score are already on rows (`detector_class`, `detector_score`); the V-1 weight knobs (`fallback_font_wght`/`fallback_cjk_scale`/`fallback_cjk_stroke`, cell 10 L305–307) provide the emphasis vocabulary for a per-region weight map in a later batch.

**Defect found (B2 risk, must fix or gate):** cell 24's sliced-mode wrapper returns `{'boxes':…, 'raw_mask':…, 'textlines': []}` (L118) — on tall strips (slice mode active) the textline quads are **discarded**, so B2's angle source would silently vanish exactly on long webtoon pages. B2 must either thread textlines through the slicer (offset-stitched per slice) or gate angle-inheritance on non-sliced pages with a loud disclosure. [FACT]

## M9-A.3 R3 — Original ink-color capture (future color manhwa/webtoon)

**Current state:** `choose_text_color` (cell 10 L632–679) samples the mean brightness of the inpainted ROI and snaps to binary black/white per region type (bubble>160→black else white, etc.) — correct for B&W manga, lossy for color art (S001_color_webtoon is archived exactly for this future).

**B3 design:** at the inpaint stage (cell 9, before inpainting), for each region sample the **median ink color from the ORIGINAL image ∩ text-pixel mask ∩ bbox±pad** — the mask already exists as the `text_mask` artifact (`build_page_text_mask`, cell 6 L769–858: CTD raw mask → threshold → close → clean → adaptive dilate → clip-to-regions+10 px) — and store `ink_color` (BGR) on the detections/ocr/translation rows. At render time use `ink_color` with a **contrast guard**: if its luminance distance from the local inpainted background falls below a threshold, snap to the binary black/white (the `choose_text_color` decision) — never render unreadable text. B&W manga naturally samples near-black (artist ink ≈ 0.17–0.23 fill, dark pixels) so current appearance is preserved by construction; empty/failed mask → fallback to `choose_text_color`, loud disclosure in render meta. [FACT + design]

## M9-A.4 N-1 disclosure — `get_font` silent degradation hole

`get_font` (cell 10 L179–214) wraps `ImageFont.truetype(path, size)` in try/except and on failure falls back — L204–210: truetype(path) → except → truetype(regular_path) → except → **`ImageFont.load_default()` with NO warning and NO log_event**. A corrupt/HTML/missing font file (or a path pointing at a failed download) silently degrades every render to the bitmap default font — Bengali becomes tofu/blank with zero diagnostics. The probe session hit this class **live**: `m009_work/fonts/NotoSansBengali-Regular.ttf` and `-Bold.ttf` turned out to be HTML error pages (failed downloads; PIL raises `OSError: unknown file format`), only the VF file is a real font (sha256 `dcd42978094e584a849c84a51450eeac40c8826057d566ea6d4b9627a403a05a`). Cell 4 §8.6 (RF-13) already sanity-checks the main font at provisioning; the remaining hole is `get_font`'s silent arm. **B1 opportunity (one line):** WARN + `log_event` (and optionally raise on the `load_default` arm, per the D-4 WARN-never-silent philosophy). The v2 instrument's own font resolution is loud by design (sha256 + load probe + glyph-ink scan of `ক া ি ী । ক্য`) — the N-1 pattern to copy. [FACT]

|---|---|---|---|
| **B1 fill** | cell 10 render only | (1) overflow into narrow bubble rows; (2) regression of MANGABD-004 small-bubble calibration; (3) mask failures (thought/cloud bubbles, no white interior); (4) per-line band wrap differs from `split_text_to_lines` wrapping → line-break changes on kept regions; (5) cap-bound under-fill persists (synthetic class) | band-min widths + usable-extent feasibility (proven by construction); trigger guard keeps non-triggered regions byte-equal (instrument-verified on 10/10 archived regions); mask-empty → current path fallback; guards accept size ≥ current; cap_bound disclosure + optional cap raise ruling |

## M9-A.6 Proposed batch sequence (for the PM/Owner ruling)

**B1 (fill) → B2 (orientation) → B3 (color).** Rationale: B1 is the Owner's #1, is self-contained in cell 10's renderer (no detection/inpaint/OCR surface), already has ruling-grade before/after numbers on the archives (this record), and its trigger guard makes it safe to ship incrementally; B2 touches the detection data model (detections.json schema + the cell-24 slicer defect) and should follow once B1's render surface is stable; B3 is future-facing (color manhwa) and depends on the same data-model extension, so it lands last with S001_color_webtoon as its acceptance fixture. Each batch keeps the Phase C discipline: cells-scoped patches, byte-parity proofs for untouched surfaces, instruments committed, Flash DELTA gate, Owner live gate.

## M9-C.2 C-1 (Phase C discovery) — cells 11/12 V-3 wrapper forward-threading

The V-3 vertical wrapper that cells 11/12 install around `render_bengali_text` sits IN `render_page`'s call chain in every fresh sequential run. Its signature lacked `fit_result`, so without threading, **every bubble render after cell 12 would TypeError** (render_page's except would silently skip the region — a catastrophic regression class the static gates would never catch at the cell level). Fix: both wrappers (cell 11 L300–309 / cell 12 L255–264) gain `fit_result=None` and forward it verbatim on the fallback arm; **the vertical arm is byte-untouched** (the decision's `vertical_note` guard — the identical predicate — guarantees no vertical-class region ever carries a fit_result in production). Scope extension beyond the ruling's "cell 10 + 9 + 6 if needed" is disclosed here: 8 diff lines per cell, every changed line proven part of the wrapper patch (verify V1i). [FACT]

## M9-C.3 Default-ON decision (disclosed deviation from the Phase A preview)

Phase A M9-A.1.6 sketched the production path "config-gated, default OFF" — written BEFORE the ruling as a safety posture. The ruling approves trigger/cap/target as **production parameters** and the batch's purpose is the Owner's visible live-gate improvement; a default-OFF gate would make the Owner's live render show zero change. Decision: `bubble_fill_enable` default **True** (the approved behavior active), with the knob as the documented rollback switch — proven: `enable=False` reproduces the PRE render **byte-exact** on both archives (verify V5h). Flash Phase D is asked to specifically review this decision. [DECISION + TEST RESULT]

## M9-B2-A.0 Scope, authorization, and deliverables

**Directive** (Owner authorization per Ruling 1 batch sequence — B2 = orientation inheritance; PM relay zai-web IM 2026-10-27): investigation + design ONLY, docs + instrument, stop for the PM/Owner ruling before implementation. Problem statement: Bengali always renders axis-aligned (angle=0.0) even when the original Japanese was tilted/slanted/emphasized — the artist's visual intent is lost. Desired: inherit the original textline orientation (tilt + emphasis) so diagonal/shouted lines carry matching energy. Investigation tasks: (1) detection side, (2) rendering side, (3) vertical-path protection, (4) geometry math, (5) edge cases. Deliverables: code-path map with citations, geometry proof, risk table, proposed batch scope, committed measurement instrument (angle distribution on archived S003/S007, classification bands truly-vertical 85-95° / tilted 5-85° / axis-aligned <5°, per-region angle + classification). **This commit touches exactly 3 files:** this record (+ CURRENT PHASE entry), `scripts/m009_b2_orientation_measure.py` (the committed instrument), `scripts/m009_b2_orientation_results.json` (its committed results). The notebook is byte-untouched (sha256 `0858a1c9…` == `f218b35`'s); samples/models untouched. [FACT]

## M9-B2-A.1 Detection side — code-path map (task 1)

**Where quads exist and where they die.** CTD textline objects carry `.pts` quads; the fallback loop reads them at cell-06 L578 and immediately collapses each to an axis-aligned bbox at **L585-588** (`x1=min(pts[:,0]) … y2=max(pts[:,1])`), emitting box dicts `{x,y,w,h,score,detector_class}` (L596-603) that carry **no angle and no quad**. The raw `textlines` list IS returned alongside (L608-612) — but it never reaches the region model. [FACT, static]

**Who owns the regions.** `detect_and_render`-class orchestration (cell-06 L1076-1160): RT-DETR is the primary (`rtdetr_detect` L1076 → text_bubble/text_free candidate boxes L1089); **CTD is fallback-only for boxes** (L1096: only if RT-DETR finds nothing) and otherwise runs solely for the pixel mask (L1113-1138, `use_ctd_pixel_mask`), where its `textlines` (L1124) feed only `adaptive_dilate_mask` (L813/L850). The Owner's workflow (all archived detections: detector_class ∈ {text_bubble, text_free}) therefore NEVER produces CTD-class regions — the archived `angle` values are all 0.0 and the archived `quad` values are all bbox-reconstructed (verified in all 6 archives). [FACT, static + archive]

**Where the region angle is born — as a constant.** Region build at cell-06 L1170-1212: `quad = np.array([[x,y],[x+w,y],[x+w,y+h],[x,y+h]])` (L1190-1195, reconstructed from the bbox) and **`angle = 0.0` hardcoded at L1197**, written into every region dict at L1206-1207 (`"quad": quad.tolist()`, `"angle": angle`). The data-model slots ALREADY EXIST and serialize into detections.json as-is (L1234+ `save_json_artifact(page_id, "detections", regions)`) — B2 needs zero schema change, only real values for the tilted class. [FACT, static]

**The cell-24 slicer (tall-strip path).** `rtdetr_detect` slice wrapper L97-106 (boxes offset+deduped, axis-aligned — no geometry to lose); `ctd_detect_boxes_and_mask` slice wrapper L108-118 returns **`'textlines': []` at L118** — on sliced webtoon strips, CTD textline quads are gone before region build could use them. Consequence: any CTD-quad-based angle source silently no-ops on the Owner's webtoon use case (MANGABD-001's original target). A pixel-based angle source computed at region-build time (post-slicer, from the region bbox on the full-res image) is immune by construction. [FACT, static]

**Region classification for emphasis.** `classify_region_from_detector` (cell-06 L377-396): text_bubble → bubble; text_free → heuristic (narrator/sfx/overlay). region_type later drives bold/stroke (see M9-B2-A.6). [FACT, static]

## M9-B2-A.2 Rendering side — the dormant machinery (task 2)

**The render chain is fully angle-wired and asleep.** `render_page` (cell-10 L1435) reads `angle = float(row.get("angle", 0.0))` at **L1518**, passes it to `bubble_fill_decision` (L1569-1579, whose guard chain returns `mode "current"` with guard `rotated` at |angle|>1 — L916, so B1 smart-fill and B2 tilt are disjoint by construction) and to `render_bengali_text` (L1617-1624). `render_bengali_text` (L1135, `angle=0.0` default at L1142) gates at **L1200: `abs(float(angle)) > 1.0` → `_render_rotated_text`** (any angle, not 90°-only). [FACT, static]

**`_render_rotated_text` supports ARBITRARY angles already** (L1304-1413): transparent RGBA canvas `max(w+4·pad, 200) × max(h+4·pad, 100)` (L1328-1335), per-line centered draw with the MANGABD-008 D-3 per-run glyph fallback (L1344-1386), then **`text_canvas.rotate(angle, expand=True, resample=BICUBIC)`** (L1388-1392) — PIL rotates any float angle — and paste centered on the region bbox center (`paste_x = center_x − rotated.width//2`, L1394-1400). Exception fallback re-renders axis-aligned (L1404-1413). The B1 defensive gate (L1172-1177) also drops any mode-"mask" `fit_result` when |angle|>1 — the two batches cannot interfere. [FACT, static]

**Paste-centering is already exact for rotated rectangles.** Any rotated rectangle is centrally symmetric; its 4-corner centroid and its axis-aligned bbox share the same center (both invariant under 180° point reflection through the rectangle's center). Therefore `_render_rotated_text`'s bbox-center paste == quad-center paste: **B2 needs no placement change.** [FACT, geometric proof]

**The one real renderer gap — fit dims.** The rotated branch fits text with `fit_font_size(text, w, h)` (L1183-1189) using the **bbox** dims. For a W×H strip tilted by t the bbox is bw = W|cos t| + H|sin t|, bh = W|sin t| + H|cos t| (proved numerically, self-test V4 5/5) — e.g. 200×60 at 20° becomes bbox 208×125 = **2.08× the strip's true height** (V5), so status-quo fitting oversizes text ~2× on tight diagonals. Bbox→(W,H) inversion (solve the 2×2 linear system) is singular at t=45° (det = cos 2t) — unreliable. **Fix: fit to QUAD extents** (W,H from the quad, the exact `crop_region_rotated` convention cell-06 L944-952), computed in `render_page`'s tilted branch from `row["quad"]` (already present in the DataFrame row) — a render_page-local change, no signature/threading changes, cells 11/12 untouched. [FACT + TEST RESULT]

**OCR side — also pre-built and dormant.** `ocr_region` (cell-07 L324) reads the region angle (L342) and at **`abs(angle) > 5.0` uses `crop_region_rotated`** (L346-347), else `crop_bubble_with_context`; the M6-B1 adapter-crop block repeats the same gate verbatim (L507-514). `crop_region_rotated` (cell-06 L935-970) warps the region quad to its (W,H) extent via `cv2.getPerspectiveTransform` + `warpPerspective` (L964-965) — a true quad in the region dict deskews the OCR crop automatically; the bbox-quad (today) would make it a no-op-ish axis crop. Note: the deskew branch skips the context padding of the flat branch (padding=4 inside the warp vs context_ratio=0.15) — a disclosed behavior delta for tilted regions only. The dormant 5.0° gate matches the Owner's axis-aligned band boundary exactly. [FACT, static]

**Inpaint mask is angle-blind — B2 cannot touch inpaint bytes.** `build_page_text_mask` (cell-06 L769-852) uses CTD raw mask + `adaptive_dilate_mask` (textlines L813) clipped to region boxes+10 (L815-825) + V-2 vpad for tall-narrow regions (L827-843) — every input is keyed on x/y/w/h or pixel masks, none on angle/quad. Storing real angles changes NO mask byte → inpainted.png unchanged → `choose_text_color` (cell-10 L1037) unchanged. [FACT, static]

## M9-B2-A.3 Vertical-path protection (task 3)

**The V-3 claim.** The cells-11/12 wrapper (cell-11 L296-311; cell-12 mirror) intercepts a render call iff `text.strip() and w < 90 and h > 2*max(1, w) and abs(float(angle)) <= 1.0` (L305) and routes it to `_qc_render_vertical` (L250-293: fit on swapped (h,w), canvas (h,w), per-line horizontal draw with CJK fallback runs, `rotate(vertical_rotate = −90)` L289-290, paste centered L291-292). B1's `bubble_fill_decision` carries the byte-copied predicate as guard `vertical_note` (cell-10 L919). [FACT, static]

**Truly-vertical vs tilted — the decision predicate.** The instrument's corpus measurement settles it: standard manga bubbles contain VERTICAL Japanese (5/6 unique regions measure |tilt| 82-95° — R1 the V-3 note at 90.07°/aniso 0.039; R6 a 131×204 bubble at 89.24°/aniso 0.30, high-confidence). If B2 stored the RAW tilt there, the dormant render gate (|angle|>1) would rotate every standard bubble ~90° and the OCR gate (>5) would warp every crop — destroying the live-accepted pipeline. Therefore: **vertical-class regions (and the V-3 narrow class) MUST keep angle 0.0.** The Owner's classification bands protect them: truly-vertical (85-95°) is a non-inheriting class; the V-3 predicate (w<90 ∧ h>2w) additionally protects narrow verticals REGARDLESS of measured angle; and the V-3 wrapper's own `|angle| <= 1.0` clause can never see a non-zero vertical angle because production never stores one. **Tilted (5-85°) = the ONLY inheriting class**, and only when the confidence gates pass. [FACT + TEST RESULT]

## M9-B2-A.4 Geometry math (task 4)

**Angle from quad (4 corners).** CTD textline quads are ordered tl, tr, br, bl in the textline's reading frame. The reading direction is the LONG side: d = (p1−p0) if |p1−p0| ≥ |p3−p0| else (p3−p0) — aspect-invariant (horizontal lines read along the top edge, vertical textlines along the left edge). θ = atan2(d_y, d_x) in image coordinates (y down). **Fold:** a textline axis has no arrow (0 ≡ 180): signed tilt t = ((θ+180) mod 180) − (180 if >90) ∈ (−90, 90] — this also resolves the "backwards text" edge case geometrically (170° ≡ −10° visual tilt). Self-test V1: synthetic quads at 0/±12/±17.5/45/170° and tall-rect cases recovered with worst error **0.0°**. [TEST RESULT]

**Pixel form (PCA/moments).** On ink-pixel coordinates, θ = 0.5·atan2(2μ11, μ20−μ02); eigenvalue ratio λ2/λ1 (anisotropy) measures orientation certainty — near-isotropic ink clouds (multi-line square bubbles) are UNSTABLE and must be gated (λ2/λ1 > 0.70 → low confidence; measured R3/R4 at 0.721, R5 at 0.631 — see M9-B2-A.5). [FACT]

**Sign convention — the PIL proof (self-test V2).** PIL `Image.rotate(β)` rotates CCW visually. Measuring a β-rotated render with the PCA estimator returns θcw = −β (worst |Δ| = **0.44°** over β ∈ {−25, −10, +10, +25}, real NotoSansBengali glyphs). Convention table: **θcw = image-coords principal-axis angle (positive = visually clockwise); pil_angle = −θcw.** Production `region["angle"]` must store **pil_angle** so the existing chain (L1518 read → L1200 gate → L1388 `rotate(angle)`) reproduces the artist's tilt with zero renderer changes. [TEST RESULT]

**Rotation + paste transform.** `_render_rotated_text` renders the block axis-aligned on an oversized transparent canvas, rotates the CANVAS (expand=True grows it to the rotated extents), and pastes centered on the region center (L1394-1400). For any rotated rectangle, corner-centroid == bbox center (M9-B2-A.2 proof) ⇒ the paste lands the rotated block exactly on the strip's center. The 4·padding canvas margin absorbs BICUBIC AA fringe. Nothing about this path needs extension for 0-360° — PIL already handles it; the only required input is the correctly-signed angle. [FACT + geometric proof]

**Deviation-from-nearest-cardinal (the Option-A semantic).** dev = t − 90·round(t/90) ∈ (−45, 45]. Rationale: near-0 measurements pass through unchanged; near-90 measurements (standard verticals AND their noisy 82-85° neighborhood) collapse to small leans that the <5° snap absorbs; genuine diagonals (30-60°) inherit a readable lean ≤45° while preserving the energy. Option B (raw tilt, ±60° cap) inherits the full diagonal instead — manga SFX convention — but would render a noisy high-confidence 84° vertical bubble at −84° (sideways); the R3 artifact (θ=82.11°, low-confidence only because anisotropy saved it) is the live proof of the hazard class. **Recommendation: Option A.** [FACT + TEST RESULT]

## M9-B2-A.6 Emphasis/weight emulation (task 2, second half)

Today emphasis is REGION-TYPE-driven, not angle-driven: `render_bengali_text` defaults bold to `region_type in ["sfx", "narrator"]` (cell-10 L1163-1164; render_page L1557) and stroke width via `choose_stroke_width(region_type)` (L1087, wired L1166-1167/L1559). Shouted SFX boxes (text_free → heuristic sfx) therefore already render bold+stroked; bubbles render Regular. **B2 options:** (a) keep emphasis exactly region_type-driven — tilted bubbles inherit the tilt but not extra weight (RECOMMENDED: zero change to live-accepted output; the Owner's O-1/O-2 backlog already tracks true emphasis cases); (b) angle-triggered emphasis: tilted-class regions get bold and/or +1 stroke via an `b2_emphasis_boost` knob default OFF — a pure addition, byte-identical at default, decided by the ruling (RB2-6). Note the renderer's rotated branch already honors bold/stroke params (L1215, L1363-1378) — option (b) would be a one-line knob read, no structural work. [FACT, static]

## M9-B2-A.7 Edge cases (task 5)

**Mixed orientation in one bubble** (multiple textlines at different angles): the renderer draws ONE block at ONE angle (block-level rotation, L1388). Pixel-PCA measures the whole ink cloud — staggered multi-line bubbles produce near-isotropic clouds → anisotropy gate → low confidence → 0.0 (R3/R4/R5 are live examples). The CTD-quad path would allow per-textline median + IQR disclosure, but is rejected as the primary source (M9-B2-A.1). Proposal: single PCA angle + confidence gates; mixed-bubble behavior = safe 0.0 default. **Very small angles (5-10°):** production snaps <5° to 0.0 (Owner band); 5-10° is a visible italic-like tilt and IS inherited. The snap also neutralizes the dormant sub-band 1-5° (render gate at 1.0° would otherwise fire on a 3° wobble with no OCR benefit — the two dormant gates become one semantic boundary at 5.0°). **Backwards text (180-270°):** the mod-180 fold maps it to its visual tilt (180 ≡ 0, 170 ≡ −10); truly upside-down glyph ORDER cannot be fixed by rotation (requires a 180° flip = mirrored order) — out of B2 scope; the confidence gates + the honest log handle it (a genuine upside-down region measures near-cardinal → stores 0.0). **Tiny regions:** <30 ink px unmeasurable → 0.0 (R-class guards unchanged). **Color pages:** the ink extractor thresholds dark pixels with an Otsu fallback, but the corpus provides NO color-page evidence — disclosed; the Phase C battery should add a synthetic color test if the Owner's live pages are colored. [FACT + DESIGN]

## M9-B2-A.10 Ruling surface (for the PM/Owner ruling)

- **RB2-1 Angle semantic:** (A) dev-from-nearest-cardinal ∈ (−45,45] — RECOMMENDED (all measured verticals → ≤8° leans, mostly snapped; diagonals inherit readable leans) vs (B) raw tilt capped ±60 (full-diagonal SFX convention; the 82-85° noise hazard renders sideways if confidence gates ever pass one).
- **RB2-2 Snap threshold:** store 0.0 below 5.0° (Owner band; aligns the dormant OCR 5.0 gate and the render 1.0 gate into one semantic boundary) — RECOMMENDED — vs store raw (let the existing 1.0/5.0 gates govern a 1-5° sub-band).
- **RB2-3 Angle source:** pixel-PCA at region build — RECOMMENDED (slicer-proof, RT-DETR-compatible, artist-ink fidelity) vs CTD-quad (dies on sliced strips; needs cell-24 fix + region↔textline mapping) vs hybrid (CTD primary + pixel fallback; complexity for no measured benefit).
- **RB2-4 Quad policy:** true quad for tilted-class confident regions only (activates OCR deskew via the existing dormant gate) — RECOMMENDED — vs bbox-quad always (no OCR change; B2 render-only).
- **RB2-5 Rotated-branch fit dims:** quad extents — RECOMMENDED (2.08× bbox overshoot measured; inversion singular at 45°) — vs bbox dims (status quo; oversize on tight diagonals).
- **RB2-6 Emphasis:** keep region_type-driven bold/stroke — RECOMMENDED — vs angle-triggered `b2_emphasis_boost` knob (default OFF).
- **RB2-7 Scope + default:** cells {6, 10} only, `b2_orientation_enable` default True with rollback knob (B1 precedent; archives byte-parity proven 0/36 rows) — RECOMMENDED.

## M9-B2-C.1 Cell-6 implementation (detection side, RB2-1..RB2-4)

## M9-B2-C.6 Disclosures

1. **Phase A's `row["quad"]` claim was WRONG** (M9-B2-A.2's "already present in the DataFrame row") — cell-07 `build_ocr_row` (L962-1004) whitelists `angle` but NOT `quad`, so translation_df rows cannot carry quads. Cell 7 is byte-untouched per RB2-7, so the in-scope fix: render_page loads the quads from the **detections artifact** (`load_json_artifact`, defined cell-03 L755, called with the exact cell-06 form) keyed by region id. `angle` needed no such fix (the whitelist already carries it — B2's tilt flows to the renderer with zero cell-7 changes).
2. **Knob placement** deviates from the Phase A sketch (§M9-B2-A.9 placed it in cell-10 §2): cell 6 executes BEFORE cell 10, so the detection-side gate must be defined in cell 6 (first-run correctness; a §2 definition would NameError-no — silently default-True — on a fresh kernel, computing angles against the Owner's intent). The render-side mirror covers the re-render-without-re-detect workflow. Both disclosed for Flash review.
3. **est_size tracking** for tilted rows now fits to quad extents (was bbox) so the render meta logs the actually-rendered font size — a meta-honesty delta for tilted rows only.
4. **Meta keys are conditional on non-zero counts** (detect `b2_tilted_regions`, render `b2_orientation`): kill-switch OFF, all-axis-aligned pages, and ALL archived pages produce byte-exact PRE stage metas.
5. **OCR deskew activation** (RB2-4 consequence, cell-07 untouched): tilted regions now carry true quads, so cell-07's dormant `abs(angle) > 5.0` gate warps tilted crops via `crop_region_rotated` — tilted Japanese OCRs deskewed (improvement intent); the deskew branch skips the flat branch's context padding (padding=4 vs context_ratio=0.15 — the M9-B2-A.2 disclosure, now live for the tilted class only).
6. **Test-fixture lesson** (no production impact): PIL `Image.rotate(expand=True)` defaults `fillcolor=0` (BLACK) — white-canvas fixtures grew black corner triangles that PCA read as the dominant "ink" (204K px, θ 169.6°). Fixed with `fillcolor=255` in all three fixtures; production crops come from real page pixels and are unaffected. Disclosed so Flash understands the fixture pattern.
7. **Emphasis skipped** (RB2-6): bold/stroke stay region_type-driven, byte-untouched; the O-1/O-2 backlog still tracks true emphasis cases.
8. **The stored angle vs the quad serve different consumers by design**: the angle is the dev (render lean, Option A), the quad is the raw ink rectangle (OCR deskew + fit dims) — e.g. a 70° strip stores angle −20° with a 70° quad: OCR reads the true deskewed strip, the Bengali renders at the −20° lean.

## M9-B2-RF14.4 Root cause + the B1-control reframe

`render_page`'s render-stage cache has no invalidation for "cached render was BLANK (rendered_regions == 0) but the page NOW has renderable Bengali rows". The trap needs (i) a pre-translation render (marks render done with a blank final) and (ii) a later resume-aware `force=False` render. **B2's Phase D merge-gate procedure manufactures (i) by design** ("DIAGONAL page from a live chapter → axis-aligned page byte-unchanged → kill-switch rollback WITHOUT re-detect" — all pre-translation renders), so the Owner's eyeball→apply→step_4 sequence deterministically ends in cached blanks. The PRE-B1 control proves the code defect is latent and version-agnostic — B1's Owner-live flow (apply THEN render) never armed it, which is exactly why B1 "rendered the full chapter correctly" and B2 "regressed": **B2 changed the workflow (the gate's eyeball renders) and shipped without the guard; the honest framing is a latent cache trap exposed by the B2 gate workflow.** The fix therefore belongs to B2's render cell (10) — and the merge-gate instructions that depend on pre-translation renders are now safe to follow verbatim. [FACT + TEST RESULT]

**Convergence with Flash's Owner-directed repro (`2a37127`, delivered mid-RF-14, docs + repo-external instruments only — no notebook bytes):** Flash's verbatim render_page sandbox reached the same wall from the other side — zero-ink does NOT reproduce from any single-call render state (S1-S7 all ink), and the Owner's symptom reproduces via "the resume trap (render checkpoint + blank final => 'Render cached' returns mean-255 blank, stage stays done; render_page silently saves a blank final + marks render done on any first pass with 0 renderable rows — poisoning every resume-aware Run All)" — the SAME trap my owner_session harness reproduces (12/12 zero-ink @ fd8d625 AND @ 5fe8804-PRE-B1). Flash's ruling (no revert, no re-gate; fix-in-place, cell-10-only) is exactly what this RF-14 delivers; the two investigations agree on the first failing decision (the cache return), on the suspects (Flash's per-region decision dump clean — no exception/off-page-paste/fit-guard trip; my (a)-(e) dispositions), and on keeping the merge gate. The RF-14 commit rebases on top of `2a37127` (disjoint file sets: Flash touched GLM_5_3_FLASH.md + scripts/m009b2_zeroink_*; this commit touches the notebook + GLM_5_3.md + scripts/m009_b2_rf14_*). [FACT]

## M9-B2-RF14.7 Disclosures + open observations

1. **The bug is NOT in the B2 angle/quad machinery** — every B2 surface (estimator wiring, mirror, fit dims, rotate/paste, Tests 16-18) is byte-untouched by this fix; the root cause is the B1-era render cache interacting with B2's own gate procedure (M9-B2-RF14.4's reframe).
2. **O-RF14-1 (open)**: estimator tilt-leak on horizontal-class bubbles — the ±2px crop's ink (bubble border ring + text + context art) can measure dev > 5° on a horizontal bubble (+5.26° observed) and store a bogus lean. Renders still ink; quality issue. Candidate bounded RF (ring-aware ink extraction / stricter aniso-vs-ring gate); needs an Owner ruling — deliberately NOT fixed here (scope discipline: zero-text root cause only).
3. **O-RF14-2 (open)**: tilted-class measurement accuracy — a true −18° bubble stored −6.72° (ring mass pulls PCA toward 0). Same family as O-RF14-1.
4. **O-RF14-3 (observation)**: the pre-translation eyeball render marks render-done with `rendered=0` silently at THAT moment (legitimately untranslated rows → no WARN). The fix catches the trap at re-render time; a future hardening could add a loud "rendered 0 regions" hint at mark time (disclosed, not implemented — minimal-fix discipline).
5. **Harness env divergences (all disclosed in M9-B2-RF14.1)**: torch stub, pathlib root remap, cell 00 side-effect seeding, cell 5/23 in the exec set, synthetic inpaint, stubbed OCR/detector inference. None touch cell bytes; the battery + archives + sim provide the byte-level safety net around these divergences.
6. **Battery count changes 18/18 → 19/19** — the merge-gate text for the Owner live run must read cell 10 **19/19** (Test 19 added; Tests 1-18 byte-identical).
7. **The Phase D merge-gate instructions are now safe to follow verbatim** (eyeball renders no longer poison step_4); the kill-switch rollback step behaves identically (cache-hit on a healthy render was already the byte-exact rollback; a blank-cached page now re-renders instead of silently returning nothing).
8. **Fix-mechanics delta vs Flash's `2a37127` proposal (disclosed for the Owner's ruling)**: Flash sketched the RF-14 candidate as "never save blank final + mark done on rendered_count==0 with rows present (loud named failure)" plus a byte-size suffix on the 'Render cached' line. This RF-14 instead (a) KEEPS the blank-save semantics (a legitimately-untranslated page may hold a final == inpainted copy; refusing to save would turn the gate's own pre-translation eyeball renders and any all-marker page into named failures), and (b) kills the poisoned-cache RETURN — the state that actually produced the Owner's zero-text — with a loud bypass + WARN + reset_page, machine-guarded by battery Test 19 (arms the trap, asserts the bypass + ink, asserts healthy-cache byte-stability). Net effect: Flash's ruling intent (no silent zero-text; loud lines; cell-10-only; no revert/re-gate) is fully met, 'Render cached' becomes a SAFE line to see, and the Owner's manual 'instant restore' step (reset_page from_stage=render) is no longer needed — though it remains valid on pre-fix tips. Flash's never-save-blank variant and the cached-line byte-size observability add remain available as a follow-up ruling if the Owner prefers those mechanics.
